The saver's `FUNDING_ANALYSIS_SUBTYPE` map (`src/underwrite/funding-letter-pdf.mjs`) listed four types, so `analysisTypeOf()` returned null for that fifth file and the loop skipped it without a log, a throw or a counter — while the delivery email promises it as item 5 (`src/messaging/templates/u02-funding-delivery.html:40`). The map now carries five, `funding_summary` → subtype `funding_summary`, titled "Capital Readiness Summary" (the vendor renderer's own title for that type, `render-pdf.js:955`); the filename fallback recognises both `funding_summary.pdf` and `Capital-Readiness-Summary.pdf`; the repair pack's own `repair_plan_summary` is deliberately NOT recognised, because it does not belong on the funding stack. No migration: `documents.subtype` is not constrained by the database and `src/documents/kinds.mjs` says so in its own header. MEASURED, not asserted — a scratch Postgres with all 239 migrations applied to an empty database, a client carrying the repo's own `academy` simulated credit file (`scripts/sim/push-credit.mjs`, which tiers FULL_FUNDING), the REAL `buildLetterPackForClient` and the REAL `persistFundingLetterFiles`: before the change the pack carried five analysis files and **four** rows landed in `documents`; after it, **five** — `bank_lender_match_list`, `credit_analysis_report`, `credit_optimization_roadmap`, `funding_snapshot`, `funding_summary`, the last one titled Capital Readiness Summary with real bytes behind it. It passes both with the Python printer and with the short pdf-lib fallback. WHAT THIS DOES NOT FIX, stated so nobody reads it as finished: F42 — the live database still holds zero deliverable documents for any client, all time. This makes the saver correct; it does not prove anything ever reaches a real client. And a MANUAL_REVIEW client still gets no Capital Readiness Summary at all, because `buildDocuments()` emits `hold_notice` + `operator_checklist` for that tier and neither is on the funding stack — existing behaviour, watched happen, not changed here. `PRINTER_FILES` in `src/underwrite/black-report-pdf.mjs` was NOT expanded and is not the cause: the printer produces exactly the four it lists, and the fifth file never passes through it. (2) STORED HTML NO LONGER RENDERS ON THE APP'S OWN ORIGIN. Contracts are stored as `text/html` (`src/contracts/send.mjs:50`), and `api/documents/[id].mjs` served that content type with no `Content-Disposition`, so opening a stored contract rendered it as a page on the same origin whose localStorage holds the session token `fh_token` (`public/app/client-portal.html:2049`) — while `src/lib/render-template.mjs:46` is `return String(val);`, no escaping at all, over 252 CRM merge fields. One hostile string typed into a client's name field turned "open the contract" into session theft, staff included. There was no Content-Security-Policy anywhere in this repository. When, and only when, the resolved mime type is `text/html` the route now also sends `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'` and `Content-Disposition: attachment`. PDFs and images are byte-for-byte unchanged and still open inline. The signed-link auth is untouched: a wrong signature, an expired link, a signature minted for a different document and an unknown id are all still the same 404, and a missing secret is still 503. THE ONE BEHAVIOUR CHANGE FOR A PERSON: a member of staff opening a stored HTML contract from the Documents screen now gets a saved `.html` file instead of a rendered page. The client's own signing flow does NOT use this route — `/contract.html` reads the body as JSON from `/api/contracts/sign` — so signing is unaffected. PROVED: ten cases in `src/http/documents-html-guard.pg.test.mjs` against real Postgres and the real Netlify handler, and a negative control was run — with the two guard calls removed, three of the ten fail. TEST RESULT: `npm run lint` clean, 1794 files. Unit half 8729 tests / 8726 pass / 0 fail / 3 skipped (baseline 8721/8718/0/3; the 8 extra are the new unit tests). Database half on a freshly created and migrated scratch Postgres: 2292 tests, 15 failures — and `origin/main` measured the same way on its own fresh scratch database gives 16, whose names are a strict superset: every one of the branch's 15 is on main, and main additionally failed `the rule snapshot means a later rate change cannot restate history` (`src/affiliates/economics.pg.test.mjs`), which this diff does not touch. 2026-09-05 | repair-letter-send | CORRECTION 2, AND THE FIX FOR IT: A LETTER THE MAIL COMPANY HAD ALREADY TAKEN COULD BE SENT A SECOND TIME. WHAT I TOLD YOU BEFORE THAT WAS WRONG: the two entries below say "mailing the same dispute letter twice is now impossible". That word was false, and I am not going to use it here. THE DEFECT: the whole guard rests on writing down "this letter was mailed" the moment the mail company takes it. The code only wrote that down when the mail company also handed back an ID number for the letter. The mail company can accept a letter and answer with no ID. When that happened the envelope went out and our records said nothing had. The letter then showed up on the staff list of "stuck" letters, a staff member could release it, and it went out again — a second envelope to a real person and a second bill. The same thing happened if the database hiccuped for a moment during that one write, because that write's error was thrown away without a word to anyone. MEASURED ON A REAL DATABASE ON 2026-09-05, before the fix: press one, the mail company said yes with no ID, one envelope, our row said "not mailed"; the row was then offered to staff as stuck; the release worked; press two, a second envelope. WHAT THE CODE DOES NOW. (1) THE FACT THAT MATTERS IS THAT THE MAIL COMPANY TOOK IT, not that it gave us an ID. The mailing is written down on a yes, ID or no ID. The ID column stays empty in that case, and empty means UNKNOWN, never "this did not happen". (2) THAT ONE WRITE IS NO LONGER SILENT. It is tried twice, and if it still cannot be written the letter's result says so, the whole send carries a list called `unrecordedMailings`, and a line goes to the log that says DO NOT RE-SEND IT. (3) THE DATABASE ITSELF NOW REFUSES TO UN-MAIL A LETTER. `db/migrations/334` adds two rules Postgres enforces on every write from now on, so a hand-typed database command is refused exactly as the app is. (4) THE LIVE SEND BUTTON now passes the mail company's own answer to "did anything actually leave this building?" through to the guard, instead of throwing it away and guessing from the wording of the error. WHAT IS STILL TRUE AND NOT FIXED, both of them stated plainly because the last two versions of this note left them out: a letter sent with no saved row has nothing to write on, so nothing stops a second one — that is unchanged behaviour and the app has always allowed an ad-hoc letter; and if the database cannot be reached for BOTH tries of that one write, our records still do not know the envelope went, so the database cannot refuse a release of it. That case is now loud instead of silent, but loud is not the same as prevented. PROVED BY RUNNING IT on a scratch database: a yes with no ID records the mailing and the letter can no longer be released or re-sent (one mail company call, not two); a database error on that write is reported instead of swallowed; a refusal that provably never reached the network still gives the letter back; a genuinely stuck letter still clears and can then be sent; and two presses at the same instant still produce exactly one envelope. | branch feat/portal-schema

2026-09-05 | repair-letter-send | CORRECTION, AND THE FIX FOR IT: THE GUARD THAT STOPS A DISPUTE LETTER BEING MAILED TWICE WAS DESTROYING LETTERS THAT WERE NEVER MAILED AT ALL. The entry below this one told Chris that when a send failed, "the row stays claimed for a person to reconcile". BOTH HALVES OF THAT SENTENCE WERE UNTRUE OF THE CODE, and the second one hid the worse half of the problem. (1) IT WAS NOT ONLY A FAILED SEND. The guard marked a letter "mailed" the moment it picked the letter up, BEFORE it called the mail company at all. So a send that the safety fence held — where nothing was transmitted and the network was provably never touched — still left the letter marked as mailed and unable to ever be sent. (2) THERE WAS NO PERSON WHO COULD RECONCILE IT, AND THE DAMAGE WAS NOT LIMITED TO THAT ONE LETTER. A brand new replacement letter for the same person, same credit bureau, same round and same destination was refused as well, because the guard counted the false mark. Nothing anywhere in this system could clear either one. The letter and every future replacement for it were dead. MEASURED ON A REAL DATABASE ON 2026-09-05, three presses, with a network call rigged to throw an error if anything ever reached it — it never fired, so nothing left the building: press one, the fence held the call and the row was marked sending with a mailing time stamped on it; press two on the same letter with the fence off was refused "already mailed" and the mail company was called zero times; press three on a brand new replacement row was refused "already mailed duplicate letter", also zero calls. A GENUINE MAIL-COMPANY OUTAGE BRICKED THE LETTER THE SAME WAY. WHAT THE CODE DOES NOW, and this is a literal description of it. THE ONE FACT IS NOW CARRIED, NOT GUESSED FROM WORDING: `src/lib/outbound-fetch.mjs`, the single place this system makes an outbound call, returns `transmitted` on every result, and every `false` is returned from a branch that sits ABOVE the call, so it is proved by which line ran and not by reading an error message. A timeout and a dropped connection both report `transmitted: true`, because the vendor may have accepted the work before the connection died. `src/messaging/providers/mail-letter.mjs` carries that up as `preTransmission` on every failure it returns, and `src/repair/send.mjs` reads that fact BEFORE it reads any message text. It used to read only the text, against a list of eight fixed phrases, and the fence's wording was not one of them. THE PHRASE LIST STILL EXISTS AND THAT IS DELIBERATE: two callers rebuild the mail result and drop the fact — the closure in `api/repair/send.mjs` and the address refusals in `src/metro2/delivery/send.mjs` — and every one of those is genuinely a refusal before transmission, so the list is what still catches them. Delete the list and they start holding letters they should release; trust the list alone and this defect comes straight back. TWO FACTS NOW LIVE IN TWO COLUMNS (`db/migrations/333_dispute_letter_send_claim.sql`): `send_claimed_at` means "this letter is taken", is stamped before the call, and RELEASES; `mailed_at` means "this letter was mailed", is stamped only after the mail company answered with an identifier, and is NEVER released by anything or anybody. There is one unique index on each. The mailing index is the hard promise — one physical mailing per case, bureau, round and destination, for ever, and nothing in the code can undo a row's membership of it. The claim index is a deliberate superset of it, which is what lets a regenerated replacement for a letter that really did go out be refused BEFORE the call rather than after an envelope is already in the post. THE STUCK STATE NOW HAS A WAY OUT: `clearStuckSendClaim()` in `src/repair/send.mjs` lets a member of staff who has checked the mail company's own records say "this one did not go", with their identifier and their written reason, both stored on the row and written to the decision log that `src/repair/lens.mjs` renders in plain English on the client's timeline. It refuses unless the row is claimed, carries no mailing, carries no mail-company identifier, and the claim is at least fifteen minutes old — the network call's own hard cut-off is ten seconds, so it cannot race a call that is merely slow. IT CLEARS THE CLAIM AND NEVER THE MAILING, so it cannot turn a letter that really went out into one that may go out again. IT CAN BE WRONG THE OTHER WAY, and that is accepted and stated: if the letter did reach the mail company and only the reply was lost, clearing lets a second one go out — which is exactly why it is a deliberate, named, recorded human act and why nothing calls it on a timer. RE-STAGING WAS NOT A WAY OUT EITHER, AND IS NOW HONEST: `src/repair/analyze.mjs` did not count a letter sitting in `sending` or `delivered` as already staged, so re-staging a round wrote a SECOND letter row, which the index then refused at send time — another dead letter, not a recovery. Both states are now counted, so a re-stage reports the round as already staged and hands back the existing letter. A DEFECT IN THE REPAIR ITSELF, FOUND BY RUNNING IT AND FIXED: the new migration originally cleared the false mailing marks while the OLD rule that required them was still in force, so `node db/migrate.mjs` against a scratch database carrying one stuck row aborted with a constraint violation and applied nothing — meaning the fix could not have reached any database that had actually been used, which is the only kind production is. The old rule is now dropped first. `src/repair/send-claim-migration-order.test.mjs` fails if that order is ever reversed. PROVED BY RUNNING IT, EVERY LINE WATCHED: on a scratch Postgres, a send the fence held releases the letter and the very next press really mails it; a genuine mail-company error keeps the claim and a retry calls nobody; a replacement letter claims cleanly after a released one; two simultaneous presses on the same letter call the mail company EXACTLY ONCE and two simultaneous presses on two duplicate rows also mail once; a stuck claim is cleared by a named person and the letter is sendable again; clearing is refused on a letter that really was mailed; and the database itself refuses to release a mailing. Separately, a scratch database was put back into the OLD shape with three real-world rows in it — one bricked, one genuinely mailed, one predating the guard — and the new migration was applied to it: the bricked row's false mailing mark was cleared and its claim preserved, the genuine mailing was left exactly as it was, the pre-guard row's unknown stayed unknown as NULL, and the bricked row was then cleared by a named person end to end. MEASURED, NOT ASSERTED: `npm run lint` clean, 1800 files. Unit half 8752 tests / 8749 pass / 0 fail / 3 skipped. Database half on a scratch Postgres created and migrated from empty for this run: 2309 tests / 2293 pass / 15 fail / 1 skip; `origin/main` at 2d81f1c5 run the identical way on its own freshly created scratch database gives 2278 / 2261 / 16 / 1, and comparing the failing test NAMES with the timings stripped there are ZERO new failures — all 15 appear on main too, and main carries one more that passes here. NOT BUILT, and said plainly: there is NO SCREEN AND NO WEB ADDRESS for clearing a stuck letter. `clearStuckSendClaim()` and `listStuckSendClaims()` are written, exported and proved, and a member of staff cannot reach either without a developer, because the files that add a web address are another lane's. The exact wiring is written into the task report. Nothing writes `mail_cost_cents` yet. | A safety guard meant to stop a person receiving two identical dispute letters was instead permanently destroying letters that were never sent, including every replacement for them, with no way for anyone to undo it. | branch feat/portal-schema

2026-09-05 | repair-letter-send (new page) | [SUPERSEDED — the "IMPOSSIBLE" in this headline was false when it was written; the 2026-09-05 entry at the top of this file says what is and is not true now] PRESSING SEND TWICE ON A DISPUTE LETTER THAT HAS A SAVED ROW MAILS IT ONCE, AND THE TWO TABLES THE CLIENT PORTAL SITS ON EXIST. THE DEFECT: `src/repair/send.mjs` set a letter to `sent` AFTER the mail provider answered, with no check of what the status already was, and `dispute_letters` carried no unique index of any kind. So posting the same send payload a second time handed the same letters to PostGrid again — two identical envelopes to the consumer, two to the credit bureau, and two bills. The only thing in the way was a disabled button in a browser, which a retry, a second tab or a command-line request walks straight past. THE FIX, IN THE DATABASE AND NOT IN THE BROWSER: `db/migrations/332_dispute_letter_mail_guard.sql` adds `mailed_at`, a `sending` status, and `uq_dispute_letters_one_mailing`, a unique index over (org, case, bureau, round, destination) covering every row that carries a `mailed_at`. `src/repair/send.mjs` now CLAIMS the letter in one statement BEFORE the provider is called, so two callers cannot both pass the check — the second one's update matches nothing and it never makes the call; a duplicate letter row for the same case, bureau, round and destination is refused by the index instead of becoming a second envelope. THE INDEX KEYS ON `mailed_at` AND NOT ON `status` ON PURPOSE: `mailed_at` is a new column, every existing row is NULL, NULL is outside the index and is never backfilled, so the guard is total from this migration forward and not one historic row of real mailings had to be rewritten to make the index build. A CLAIM THAT MIGHT HAVE TRANSMITTED IS NEVER AUTO-RETRIED — if the provider call was made and did not come back we do not know whether the letter went, so the row stays claimed for a person to reconcile; the claim is released ONLY for the fixed set of refusals that happen above the network call (no API key, no return address, no destination, no PDF or HTML, private carrier to a PO box). REFUSE ONLY ON POSITIVE EVIDENCE: a letter id naming no row is still mailed, exactly as it was before, so the guard cannot start silently dropping sends it has no evidence against. ALSO IN THIS COMMIT, the schema the portal needs: `client_waypoints` (330) is the per-client ordered checklist — whose job each step is, its state, `completed_at`, `due_at` so "overdue" is a computed fact and never a guess, and `paid_alternative_price_cents`, the column that makes it an upsell system rather than a to-do list, present from the first migration. It is a NEW table and deliberately not `tasks`: `tasks_assignee_role_ck` (041) allows staff roles only and `src/lib/create-task.mjs:70` throws because "Principal types (client, affiliate, partner) must never own internal work". `paid_service_requests` (331) is one row per "do it for me", general enough for a dispute round today and a credit pull or funding application later, storing the priced LINE ITEMS rather than a bare number — owner-set $100 flat per round covering all three bureaus, +$10 for a creditor letter, +$20 for the CFPB and state attorney general filings — with a CHECK that the lines add up to the total, and a partial unique index on (org, idempotency key) so a double press is one row, the same index `events` and `soft_pull_requests` already use. NULL SURVIVES EVERYWHERE AND ZERO IS REFUSED: a waypoint with no paid alternative stores NULL and the database refuses 0 outright, so nothing can read a zero and call it free; a due date nobody set is NULL and is never overdue; `dispute_letters.mail_cost_cents` starts NULL meaning unknown, not free. A PAID ROUND DOES NOT CONSUME A PURCHASED ROUND (owner-set): `round_no` on the new table is the self-serve counter, `repair_programs.rounds_cap` is the program counter, and there is deliberately no column joining them — a test asserts the cap does not move and that no such column exists. MEASURED, NOT ASSERTED: `npm run lint` clean, 1797 files. Unit half 8728 tests / 8725 pass / 0 fail / 3 skipped. Database half on a scratch Postgres created and migrated from empty for this run: 2300 tests / 2284 pass / 15 fail / 1 skip, and `origin/main` run the identical way on its own freshly created scratch database gives 2278 / 2262 / 15 / 1 with a BYTE-IDENTICAL list of the same 15 failing test names — no new failure. The double-mailing claim is proved by running it: the same payload posted twice calls the mailer once and leaves one letter row carrying the FIRST send's provider id; a duplicate row for the same case, bureau, round and destination is refused and keeps a NULL `mailed_at`; a pre-transmission refusal gives the letter back and it then sends; an HTTP error keeps the claim and a retry calls nothing. NOT BUILT, and said plainly: nothing writes `mail_cost_cents` yet — reading a price off the provider response is a later change, so the margin on the $100 is recordable but not yet recorded; no endpoint reads either new table and no screen exists; and a letter posted with no letter id still has no row to claim, which is unchanged behaviour and not a new hole. | Re-posting a send mailed a real person's dispute letters twice and billed twice, and this had to be closed before any client-facing "buy another round" button exists. | branch feat/portal-schema

2026-09-04 | repair-documents | CORRECTION, AND THE FIX FOR IT: THE PHOTO CLASSIFIER USED TO CALL THINGS A GOVERNMENT ID THAT THE DOCUMENT AGENT HAD JUST SAID WERE NOT ONE. The entry below this one told Chris that an unclear answer "falls back to other rather than guessing". THAT SENTENCE WAS NOT TRUE OF THE CODE. The word-match list in `src/handlers/inbound-mms-docs.mjs` ended with a bare `\bid\b`, which matches the word "id" in any sentence — including every sentence saying there is no ID in the picture. Run against the real classifier, all five of these were filed as `id_document`: "No valid ID was visible in this photo", "not an ID", "the photo is too blurry to read the ID", "a selfie, no id card", and "a driver license belonging to someone other than the client". WHY THAT IS SERIOUS: `id_document` is one leg of the identity gate in `src/inquiry-ops/doc-gate.mjs`, and that gate is what decides whether dispute letters may be posted to the credit bureaus in a client's name. A picture of nothing satisfied it, and `src/repair/handlers.mjs` then moved the client's card on to `analysis` as though their identity were proved. WHAT THE CODE DOES NOW, and this is a literal description of it: the bare `\bid\b` match is gone; a phrase is refused if it carries any denial or doubt word (no, not, cannot, unable, missing, unreadable, illegible, unclear, unknown, unsure, invalid, expired, blurry, obscured, glare, maybe, might, possibly, probably, appears, seems, looks, likely, either, or, another, someone, else, third-party, mismatch, and the rest of the list at `NEGATION_OR_HEDGE`); a phrase longer than FOUR words is refused (six let "driver license of the client's brother" and "handwritten note saying ID card" through as a government ID; four refuses both and still clears "Arizona driver license" and "state issued photo id"), and a phrase naming another person or describing the picture rather than the document is refused, because the agent is asked for the plainest NAME of the document and anything longer is a sentence about the photo; a reply whose `outcome` is anything but `accept`, or which carries a `hold_reason` or any listed issue, is refused outright, so a document the agent did not accept is never typed as a verified identity document; when the agent returns more than one phrase they must all agree on one type, and a single unreadable phrase spoils the whole reply; anything else stays `other`, exactly as it was before this batch. THE FILENAME, THE SENDER AND THE ORDER THE PICTURES ARRIVED IN ARE STILL NEVER READ. A photo that stays `other` is now recorded with `classified_by: null` instead of the agent's name, so a picture nobody could read never looks agent-verified. PROVED BY RUNNING IT, not asserted: the exported `classifyMmsImage()` was driven with each of the five sentences above and the real `checkDocPacket()` was then asked what it sees — all five now file as `other` with `docGateSeesId:false`; "Arizona driver license" files as `id_document` with `docGateSeesId:true`; "possibly a passport" files as `other` with `docGateSeesId:false`; and a `driver's license` the agent put on hold files as `other` with `docGateSeesId:false`. ALSO IN THIS COMMIT: the handler's import of the document agent was still pointing at the old `./ghl-doc.mjs`, which the GoHighLevel rename deleted — every inbound picture message would have crashed on load. It now imports `./doc-check.mjs`. THREE THINGS THIS DOES NOT FIX, written into the journey page so nobody reads it as finished: (a) WITHDRAWN AS FALSE, 2026-09-04 — an earlier draft of this entry said the document agent row carries no instructions on a freshly migrated database. It does: a virgin database with all 239 migrations applied to empty returns `DOC-CHECK | live | prompt length 3275` and classification works. The wrong figure came from querying a database the pg test half had already mutated, where the same query returns `DOC-CHECK | draft | 0`. The corrected measurement is on `docs/journeys/repair-documents-actual.md`; (b) the credit-repair contract check `canLeaveIntake()` still does not run on the enrolment path (COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED); (c) nothing moves a card out of `analysis` without a member of staff pressing the button. | The sentence Chris was shown described a refusal the code did not perform, and the wrong label landed on the gate that mails letters to credit bureaus. | pending
2026-09-04 | repair-documents (new page) | THE REPAIR DOCUMENT PATH IS CONNECTED END TO END FOR THE FIRST TIME. Three separate breaks, all in the same run of steps, none of them a missing feature — every piece already existed and none of them was wired to the next. (1) A REPAIR CLIENT COULD NOT SEE THE DOOR THEY UPLOAD THEIR ID THROUGH. `src/repair/upload-doors.mjs` opened the door carrying `id_document` and `proof_of_address` on the `funding-snapshot` entitlement alone; a repair client is granted `metro2-letter-pack`, so the only door they ever saw was "Upload your bureau response". The identity documents are the first thing the program needs and there was nowhere to send them. `lanesFromEntitlementCodes()` now returns `hasIdentityLane` = funding OR repair and `activeUploadDoors()` reads it; funding clients are unchanged; `public/app/client-portal.html`'s own copy of that rule is changed to match, and the door's label goes from "Funding documents" to "ID and personal documents" because it now serves both lanes. NO NEW DOOR, SCREEN OR TAB — the same door on the same card. (2) THE "WE NEED YOUR DOCUMENTS" STAGE HAD NEVER BEEN REACHED BY ANYONE. `awaiting_documents` is in `src/repair/pipeline.mjs`, the client sentence "Upload your ID and proof of address to continue" is in `src/repair/portal.mjs`, the 14-day chase is in `src/repair/sla.mjs`, and `src/repair/handlers.mjs` already branched on `repair.docs.needed` and `repair.docs.complete` — AND NOTHING IN THE REPOSITORY EMITTED EITHER EVENT, so every repair client since the rail was written sat on `intake` and was never asked for anything. `announceRepairDocState()` in `src/repair/handlers.mjs` is the missing emitter: it fires on `repair.enrolled`, and `onRepairDocsReceived` (registered on `docs.received` in `src/repair/register.mjs`) fires when an upload lands. THE "HAVE THEY ARRIVED" QUESTION IS NOT ANSWERED TWICE: `checkDocPacket()` in `src/inquiry-ops/doc-gate.mjs` is imported, not copied. It reads that check's `present` flags rather than its `complete` flag ON PURPOSE — `complete` also requires the signed authorization, which is its own gate on its own timeline, and this stage is only about the two images the document agent reads. (3) A TEXTED PHOTO WAS FILED AS "other". `src/handlers/inbound-mms-docs.mjs` registered every inbound picture with subtype `other`, so neither the document agent nor the document gate could tell a driver's licence from a gas bill from a photograph of a dog — a client who texted their ID had, to every check downstream, sent nothing. The image is now shown to DOC-CHECK (the same seeded agent, renamed from GHL-DOC by migration 310 that already reads these pictures, `db/migrations/114_ghl_agent_seed.sql`) and its own `documents_reviewed` answer is mapped onto the `client_upload` subtypes BEFORE the document is filed, so `docs.received` carries the right type. THE FILENAME IS NEVER READ — an MMS filename is `mms-<message id>-<n>` and carries nothing. THREE REFUSALS, EACH TESTED: a documents table that will not answer emits nothing at all (unknown stays unknown; a client is never told they have not sent something on the strength of a failed read); an upload only acts on a card sitting on `intake` or `awaiting_documents`, so a round-five client texting a bureau letter is never dragged back to `analysis`; and a photo is only typed as a government ID when the agent plainly named one (see the 2026-09-04 correction at the top of this file for exactly what "plainly" means). PROVED ON A REAL POSTGRES, not asserted: a $200 trial client enrolled on a scratch database gets `rounds_cap` 2 (owner-set, unchanged), holds only `metro2-letter-pack`, and their doors read `{funding:true, inquiry:false, bureau_response:true}` — the identity door open; `repair.enrolled` then `repair.docs.needed` are both on the events table and the card sits on `awaiting_documents`; landing the ID alone leaves it there; landing the proof of address emits `repair.docs.complete` and the card moves to `analysis`; a texted photo whose agent answer is "Arizona driver license" is written to the documents table as `subtype=id_document`, stamped as the document agent's decision. Unit half 8669 tests / 8666 pass / 0 fail / 3 skipped. Database half 2272 tests / 15 failures, byte-identical failure names to `origin/main` measured the same hour on its own scratch database — no new failure. `npm run lint` clean. | Three breaks in one line of steps meant no repair client had ever been asked for the ID and proof of address the whole program is built on. | branch feat/repair-doc-path

UNKNOWN STILL BLOCKS NOBODY, in both directions: an empty state set passes every lender exactly as an empty single state did, and a lender with a blank `eligible_states` is still included and is now LABELLED `footprint: "unknown"` rather than being called national, so a blank cell is not read as a promise. Four faults, all repaired, and every one of them REDUCES what goes out. F39 CAUSE ONE — every funnel event was written with no customer attached: all 82 `survey.submitted` rows, all 95 `entry.captured` rows and all 6 `booking.created` rows in production carried `client_id = NULL`, because `src/adapters/clickfunnels.mjs` emitted without one and that column is only ever filled by the caller. The visible cost was the never-booked chase, whose exit check reads `FROM events WHERE client_id = $1` and could therefore only ever answer "not booked"; the invisible cost was that EVERY query of that shape in the repository was blind to the whole funnel. The adapter now resolves the customer ONCE per delivery, before the first event is written, through `resolveClient` — the repository's one authority. CORRECTED 2026-09-04: the first version of this entry said every local handler for these three events already called it inside the same webhook. That is true of `survey.submitted` and `entry.captured` and NOT of the three booking events, which have no local handler at all — so a ClickFunnels delivery carrying only an appointment now calls `resolveClient` where nothing used to, which on a customer we have never seen writes a new `clients` row and, ONLY when the CRM key is set and the dry-run fence is down, syncs that contact to the CRM. No new outbound call is written in this change and the existing ADAPTERS fence still governs that one, but it is a call site that did not exist before. A resolver failure keeps today's behaviour (event written, customer null) rather than 500ing the webhook and inviting ClickFunnels to retry. F39 CAUSE TWO — ClickFunnels posts ONE WEBHOOK PER SURVEY SCREEN plus retries (82 posts for 5 real surveys), each under its own ClickFunnels submission id, so the `clickfunnels:<id>:<name>` idempotency key was distinct every time and each post started its own independent text cadence. A repeat post of the same person, same funnel, same event within six hours is now still STORED and still merged onto the customer — the later screens carry answers the first post does not have, and the "Survey Complete" stage depends on the last one, so dropping them would lose the survey — but it is handed to the workflow engine ONLY ONCE (`skipInngest`). Sixteen posts under sixteen different ids now produce exactly one run, counted at the engine itself in `src/adapters/clickfunnels.test.mjs`, not inferred from a flag this code sets on itself. `booking.created` is deliberately excluded: it already has slot-level dedupe and a second booking is a real second appointment. F39, THE HALF THAT STOPS THE RUNS ALREADY ASLEEP — every booking event those runs will wake against was written before any customer was attached, so `hasBooked` in `src/workflows/s-nobook-chase.mjs` also matches the email address (case-insensitive) and the last ten digits of the phone number carried INSIDE the event payload. Scoped to the customer's own company, which the old query was not — an email match without that scope would let one company's booking answer for another's. Proved against a real Postgres in `src/workflows/nobook-chase-historical.pg.test.mjs`, 7/7, because the fake proves the author and only the database proves the SQL. F47 — the "your call is tomorrow" reminder fired within five minutes of booking for four customers, one of whose calls was three days out, and the two-hour reminder went out sixty-eight minutes before the call. The subtraction in `src/workflows/s-04b-booking-reminders.mjs` was never wrong; nothing checked that the moment it produced was still in the FUTURE. An unreadable start time makes an Invalid Date and `sleepUntil` on one wakes immediately; a call booked twenty hours out has a "24 hours before" that is already four hours gone. Both fired at once and both said "tomorrow". A reminder whose moment has passed is now not sent late — it is NOT SENT: `reminderPlan()` refuses an unreadable or already-started appointment outright, skips the 24-hour leg for anything booked inside a day and the 2-hour leg for anything booked inside two hours, and a second check on waking refuses to fire when the clock says the moment has not arrived (a retry or a replay). Tolerance five minutes either side. The confirmation still goes in every case, because it is a receipt for what they booked and not a promise about time. THAT FIRST VERSION WOULD HAVE SWITCHED BOTH REMINDERS OFF, and it was caught and repaired on 2026-09-04 before anything shipped: the plan was worked out in the open rather than inside a recorded step, and the workflow engine re-runs everything outside a step every time it wakes a job — so at the exact moment each reminder came due, the plan was worked out again against that instant, decided the moment had just gone, and sent nothing. Every customer would have received the confirmation and neither reminder. The plan is now computed inside a step and recorded once when the booking arrives, the check made on waking is inside a step too, and `src/workflows/s-04b-booking-reminders.test.mjs` gained three REPLAY tests that drive the workflow the way the engine really drives it — many invocations with the clock moving between them. All three fail against the first version and pass against this one, which is the only reason they are worth having. F49 — the fifteen-minute handoff text arrived reading "so you're not walking in cold — link: ." `src/workflows/ai-set-04-3way-handoff.mjs` called `sendTemplated` with NO context at all while the template interpolates `{{appointment.meeting_location}}`, and an absent tag renders as the empty string. Context alone would not have been enough: ClickFunnels sets `meetingUrl: null` on every booking it takes, so the honest answer is usually "the webhook carried none". Three sources are asked in order — the booking payload, the saved `bookings` row for this appointment or the customer's most recent, and failing both the customer's own portal sign-in page — and the last always answers, so the text always carries a working web address. WHAT IT DOES NOT DO, stated plainly 2026-09-04 rather than left implied: with the wording live in the database today that address is introduced as though it were the link to join the call, and for every ClickFunnels booking the address is the customer's portal sign-in page. The corrected sentence lives in the held copy commit and in `docs/ads/sms-copy-2026-09.md`; nothing in code makes the current sentence honest, so this is named and NOT fixed. The same file also refuses an unreadable start time, and from 2026-09-04 refuses a call that has already started and a booking taken inside the last fifteen minutes — the F47 fault in its second home, which the first version only half fixed, so a booking carrying yesterday's start time used to send "Your call starts in 15 minutes" the instant it arrived. F48 — the closer's pay-link text read "Hi Sim, Fundhub Capital Academy: pay $5,000 <link>", with no sender, no reason and no reference to the call it came out of; beside a dollar amount and a payment link that is indistinguishable from a scam, which is what the owner said on receiving six copies of it. `payLinkSmsBody()` in `src/sales/closer-deck.mjs` now names the sender, the offer, the call it came from and what to do if they are not ready, and carries "Reply STOP to opt out." word for word as every other text does. ONE message either way — the duplicate-send button belongs to another lane and is untouched. F1 — the booking confirmation landed about three minutes after booking against an owner target of sixty seconds, most of it a queued row waiting for a sweep that runs every five minutes. THE SWEEP CADENCE IS NOT TOUCHED, because it is one setting for every kind of message the company sends: instead the TWO confirmation rows, and only those two, ask to be worked immediately by id through `drainMessageNow` (`src/messaging/outbox.mjs`) — the mechanism already built and switched off for exactly this. Every gate still runs inside the dispatcher: the company pause switch, the daily cap, quiet hours, opt-out, the compliance screen. Nothing here can skip one, and the sweep remains the backstop for these two rows and everything else. `src/messaging/outbox.mjs` still said in its own header that nothing called it and that the owner wanted consulting before any immediate-send path went live; on 2026-09-04 that note was replaced with what was actually decided — one caller, two rows, answering the owner's own sixty-second target — so the file and the code no longer contradict each other. THE WORDING IS DRAFTED AND HELD: `docs/ads/sms-copy-2026-09.md` carries the before-and-after of all nine texts plus one false email subject line, and `db/seed/295_sms_copy_2026_09.sql` seeds it. That seed file is the whole of the branch's LAST commit, whose subject begins "HOLD, DROP THE TIP" — identify it by being the tip, because two earlier commits in the history carry a similar "COPY SEED (HOLD)" subject and both are already reverted to nothing. It is held so the owner reads the copy before a customer does. If that commit is dropped the seed file is not in the tree at all and `docs/ads/sms-copy-2026-09.md` is the copy to read; nothing else on the branch depends on it. Not one new template key is introduced, on purpose, so every code change above is correct against the wording live in the database today and dropping that commit breaks nothing — proved by running both halves of the suite with it reverted. Every body ends `Reply STOP to opt out.`, which is exactly what every text the product already sends carries. CORRECTED 2026-09-04: it is NOT the application form's opt-out sentence, which is longer, and the earlier claim that it matched the form word for word was wrong. No message makes any claim about approval, an amount or a credit outcome. FIXTURE HONESTY: `src/workflows/s-04b-booking-reminders.test.mjs` pinned reminders against a hardcoded August date that had slid into the past, so "the reminders fired" proved nothing about a real appointment; those fixtures are now relative to now, and the workflow tests gained a clock, because a sleep that returns instantly cannot prove anything about when a message goes out. No assertion was weakened and none was removed. NOT REGENERATED, and correct that it was not: `npm run journeys` produced no diff on any of the eight pages — nothing here moves a route or a route's principal gate. What moved is how often, when and with what words a message leaves, which that generator does not model. `docs/journeys/booking-notifications-flow.md` is a new page written from the code for exactly that reason (CLAUDE.md §3a.4). | A customer receiving 51 copies of a message that was untrue when it was sent is the product failing in the one place the customer can see it, and every part of it was invisible from inside: the events looked recorded, the workflows looked registered, and the suite was green. | branch fix/r2-w11-notifications
  GAP CHECKED (CLAUDE.md §4): `docs/journeys/client-intended.md` is a route-permission map and describes no message cadence, no reminder timing and no chase, so there was nothing to reconcile and no step was invented to match the code. No route, field or dependency was added; three existing messages stopped being sent when they should not be, one gained a link it always promised, and one gained context. FOUND AND NOT FIXED, named rather than hidden: the workflow engine's fan-out in `src/events/bus.mjs` is fire-and-forget with the rejection swallowed, so a failed hand-off leaves no record anywhere — pre-existing, named as another lane's first suspect, and untouched here.
2026-09-03 | client | THE PORTAL NOW READS WHERE THE CLIENT ACTUALLY IS, WHO LOOKS AFTER THEM, AND WHETHER REPAIR IS THEIRS AT ALL — six live-walk findings, all fixed at the source rather than in the copy. F33: the screen decided its whole stage from ONE entitlement code (`funding-snapshot`), so a client who had been credit-pulled, had the call and signed their agreement was still greeted with "Your call is next" and told "we have not run those yet". `GET /api/read/portal-summary` now returns a `stage` computed from FOUR INDEPENDENT FACTS, each read from the table that owns it and none derived from another: a non-demo `crs_results` row or the analyzer flags (pull), a `call.completed` event OR a non-no-show `call_outcomes` row (call), a `contracts` row with status='signed' (agreement), a `transactions` row with status='succeeded' (payment). A client can pay before signing or sign before the pull lands, and a chain of ifs that assumes one order gets those people wrong, so `key` is simply the furthest rung reached — booked → soft_pull → call_held → agreement_signed → paid. Pinned by src/http/portal-stage.test.mjs, 7/7, including the walk's own client. F34: the advisor panel read GET /api/dashboard/client, which is staff-gated and 401s for the person whose file it is, so a client ALWAYS saw a blank name; portal-summary now carries the advisor from three sources in order — a name typed on the client record, `custom_fields.cf_funding_advisor_user_id` resolved to a staff row, or the FUNDING ADVISOR who logged a call on the file. A CLOSER IS DELIBERATELY NOT A FOURTH SOURCE: closers sell, funding advisors submit the applications, and a confident wrong name under a panel headed "Your Funding Advisor" is worse than the empty state, which now reads "Not assigned yet". No email, phone or staff id leaves that endpoint. GATED ON THE REPAIR PATH, in one new place both halves import (src/repair/on-repair-path.mjs): the active `metro2-letter-pack` entitlement (already the repair lane in src/repair/upload-doors.mjs) OR outcome_tier REPAIR_ONLY (already what src/workflows/ds-02-diy-letters.mjs refuses without). EITHER, not both — a repair buyer holds the entitlement before any pull, a DIY client holds the tier before buying anything. It FAILS CLOSED: no org, no client, or a database that will not answer is a no, and the screen's `no-repair-path` class starts ON the body so an unanswered read keeps the form shut. THE SERVER IS THE GATE, NOT THE CSS: api/consent/capture.mjs refuses a CLIENT-principal grant of kind dispute_authorization with 403 not_on_repair_path. Staff keep the ability to record an authorization given on a call — that is a person exercising judgment on a file they are looking at, and the same roles already decide whether to order the pull. F2/F31: A NEW PAYING CLIENT HAD NO WAY IN. api/soft-pull-approve.mjs creates an ACTIVE `accounts` row whose password hash is 32 discarded random bytes — a credential nobody holds, including us — and nothing then sent that person anything, so every buyer walked away with a portal account they could not open and a reset screen telling them to ask an admin. Three holes closed: the purchase path now queues EMAIL-PORTAL-MAGIC-LINK through the existing requestMagicLink (non-fatal — a mail-queue failure must not lose the consent, the identity or the checkout, so the outcome is reported in the response instead); the confirm email's magic_link context is no longer spread in CONDITIONALLY, which is what made it render `<a href=""></a>` under the sentence "Here is your link to sign in to your Fundhub portal" (renderTemplate replaces an absent tag with the empty string) — no token now means the tokenless sign-in page, a door the client can still open; and the portal itself offers a signed-out visitor "Email me a sign-in link" pointing at /portal-login.html, which already owns the whole flow, rather than a second copy of that form to keep in sync. NO NEW ISSUING MACHINERY ANYWHERE: issuePortalLinkForClient() looks the address up and calls requestMagicLink, so the 15-minute TTL, the single use, the hash-only storage and the rate limit are the ones already proven. F36: the Messages tab showed "No messages yet." twice — a second row marked `hidden` that painted anyway, because `.timeline-item{display:flex}` is an author rule and beats the browser's own `[hidden]{display:none}`; nothing ever referenced either id. F37: paintTile flipped the badge to "Included" and left the price under it reading "On your call", so a client who already owned Capital Academy saw their own product priced as a thing to ask an advisor about; the price line now repaints with the lock and keeps the original on `data-price` so a revoked grant restores it. NOT SHIPPED, NAMED RATHER THAN HIDDEN: the owner/admin "send portal sign-in link" endpoint (api/auth/send-portal-link.mjs, owner/admin, whole-route gate, 404 on another company's client) is WRITTEN AND UNROUTED — netlify/functions/api.mjs is owned by another workflow in this batch and one line (`"auth/send-portal-link": authSendPortalLink,` plus its import) is still needed; src/http/routes.test.mjs fails by name until it lands, which is the intended reminder. NOT REGENERATED, and correct that it was not: `npm run journeys` was run and produced NO DIFF on any of the eight pages. Nothing here moved a route or a route's principal gate — what moved is what two endpoints ANSWER WITH, and the consent narrowing is a business rule inside a handler, which that generator does not model (same shape as the 2026-08-31 white-label entry). 2026-08-31 | white-label | A WHITE-LABEL PARTNER NOW SEES THEIR OWN BRAND ON EVERY CRM SCREEN, NOT FUNDHUB'S. `public/app/shell.js` paints every signed-in screen from `/api/org-brand`, and that endpoint answered with the ORG row for everybody — so a partner signed in and saw Fundhub's colours, type and wordmark, which is the one thing white-label is sold to prevent. `api/org-brand.mjs` now resolves the brand from the PRINCIPAL: a partner principal reads their own `partner_brand` row through `v_partner_brand_effective`, and staff, affiliates and clients read the org row exactly as before. shell.js needed no change to whose brand it paints. THE PARTNER ID IS BOUND FROM THE SESSION AND NOWHERE ELSE — there is no `?partner_id=` on this endpoint, and a partner passing another partner's id in a query string still gets their own brand. It FAILS CLOSED to the org row on every miss: no partner id on the session, no `partners` row, no `partner_brand` row, or a partner whose org does not match the session's. `v_partner_brand_effective` LEFT JOINs and COALESCEs `ink`/`paper` to hardcoded Fundhub values, so it hands back what looks like a full answer for a partner who has saved nothing — the endpoint tests for the row itself with EXISTS rather than trusting the view. THE WORDMARK WAITS FOR APPROVAL AND NOTHING ELSE DOES: `partner_brand.approval_status` is draft/review/approved and the view ignores it completely, so the gate is applied in the handler on one field. Colours and a typeface on a partner's own screens harm nobody and an edit undoes them; a wordmark is an image and an image can carry someone else's registered trademark, so an unapproved one leaves the Fundhub default in place — which is exactly what that partner saw before this change. THE STATUS COLOURS ARE NO LONGER BRAND-DRIVEN, FOR ANYBODY: `paintBrand()` used to overwrite `--alert`, `--warn`, `--ok` and `--info` from ramp stops 0, 1, 3 and 4. Those four are read in 374 places across 43 files under `public/` and every one is a state signal — blocked, behind, healthy — in a regulated consumer-finance product. Nothing constrains a brand ramp to semantically sane stops and a real brand guideline is very often a single-hue gradient, which painted all four as shades of one colour; it had already happened once on a test tenant whose screens went entirely blue. That stayed theoretical only while Fundhub's own sensibly-chosen ramp was the only thing reaching the CRM. It is not theoretical now, and it would land on the PARTNER's own staff. `--ink`, `--paper`, `--spectrum`, `--accent`, `--logo` and both faces stay brand-driven; the four status tokens keep their `fundhub-brand.css` values. THREE PLACES STATED THE OPPOSITE DECISION AND ALL THREE ARE SUPERSEDED IN THIS COMMIT, VISIBLY RATHER THAN DELETED: `public/app/shell.js:2276`, the header of `db/migrations/128_org_brand.sql` (comment only — no SQL changed, and the file says so, because a retired rule stated as current is how the next reader gets it wrong), and `docs/BRAND-THEMING-SPEC.md`. TWO APPROACHES REJECTED AND RECORDED SO NOBODY RE-OPENS THEM: giving each partner their own org (every fulfilment read binds `org_id = staff.org_id`, so Fundhub staff would stop seeing partner clients — an outage, not an upgrade), and rewriting `v_org_brand_effective` (org-keyed, and every partner sits in the shared default org, so it returns one answer for all of them). PROVED: `src/http/org-brand-principal.pg.test.mjs` 15/15 against a real Postgres — a staff GET matched key-for-key against a direct read of `v_org_brand_effective`, partner A never receives partner B's brand, a partner with no row gets the org row whole, affiliate and client unchanged, and the draft/review/approved wordmark split. `src/ui/status-tokens-are-semantic.test.mjs` 5/5 runs the real `paintBrand` against a fake root and fails if any of the four is written again. `e2e/partner-brand-crm.spec.mjs` 5/5 in a real browser on galaxy.html, the screen a partner actually lands on, under a deliberately single-hue ramp — including a reproduction of the old paint so the evidence shows a difference and not a claim. NOT REGENERATED, and it is correct that it was not: `npm run journeys` produced no diff, because the route's principal gate did not move — `/api/org-brand` still admits staff, partner, affiliate and client. What changed is what it ANSWERS WITH, which that generator does not model. | A white-label partner signing in and seeing another company's branding on every screen is the product failing at the thing it is sold for; and shipping that without pinning the status colours would have handed a partner's own staff a screen where blocked and healthy are the same colour. | PR #322

So every label on the screen — the hero label, the eight metric-tile labels, the three money-tile labels, the offer-stack column headings, the progress-bar scale, the two panel headings, the streak cells, the leaderboard rank and sub-line, and the owed-by-you time stamp — was painting at 16px body size, the same size as the number underneath it, which is why the screen read flat. Those twenty-eight are deleted, and the screen's own label class names get the caption size (13px) back through ONE escape hatch, the shape UI-STANDARDS.md §12.7 sanctions. The remaining TWO were alive and are now left exactly as they were, which is the correction this revision makes. `body{font-size:14px}` sits OUTSIDE `.app-shell`, so the brand rule never reached it; it is the size the Search and chat overlays inherit, and raising it to 16px grew the search bar from 50px to 53px tall and the magnifier glyph from 8x21 to 10x24 — measured, so it is back at 14px. The 9.5px on `.st-row .num::before` is a pseudo-element, which `*` cannot match at all; it is the stacked column label a phone shows above each figure and it is back at 9.5px. The "not known" reason sentence (`.st-row .why`) was also pulled out of the label rule: it shares a column cell with the money figure it replaces, so shrinking it would make one column show two sizes depending on whether the data exists. | UI-STANDARDS.md §12.7 and src/ui/screen-standard.test.mjs, owner-set 2026-08-30. A size that never paints is worse than no size: the screen looks flat, and the next agent reads that as a design problem and writes more px that also do nothing. But the same reasoning applied to an element the brand rule cannot reach deletes something real, which is what the first pass at this change did in two places. Measured before and after in a headless browser at 1440x900 and 390x844, empty and with rows, with the search overlay open: every element and pseudo-element compared for font-size, letter-spacing, line-height and box. | PR #306

THE BOUNDARY IS THE PRODUCT: column names that look like identity are dropped and counted, and a surviving cell that looks like an SSN, an e-mail or a phone REFUSES THE WHOLE UPLOAD before a byte reaches storage — `decline_autopsy_rows` has no name, SSN, DOB, address, e-mail, phone or account column and no `client_id`, so there is nothing to leak. Pay first, upload second. Measured 2026-08-30 in Chromium: spotted card `none`, every neighbour `rgba(10,10,10,.04) 0 1px 2px, rgba(10,10,10,.06) 0 2px 8px`. Fixed to `box-shadow:var(--panel-shadow),0 0 0 3px var(--ink)` — the resting shadow is kept and a 3px ink ring is added on top, ink rather than `--accent` because `--accent` is `ramp[5]` and a one-hue tenant can wash it out to the board colour, while ink is the same validated hex the chosen headline counter already fills with. `.card.fh-spot:hover` is named too: `.card:hover` is declared later at equal specificity and, measured by stripping the selector out of the live CSSOM, dropped the ring the moment the pointer crossed the card. 2026-08-30 | role-funding-advisor (no journey file changed — `npm run journeys:check` reports docs/journeys up to date; the generator reads routes and gates, and this adds neither) | THE BOARD NOW ANSWERS "WHAT IS WAITING ON ME", AND THE NUMBER IT ANSWERS WITH WAS FIXED BEFORE IT WAS PUT ON SCREEN. A funding advisor with forty clients on one rail could see, per card, that a client had written and nobody had answered, and that a bank had approved with no dollar amount — and nothing anywhere added those up. The only figures on the whole screen were "— cards / — est. / — held", and `.filter-spacer{flex:1}` pushed them to the FAR RIGHT of the third bar, so the leftmost thing on every bar of this screen was a filter. UI-STANDARDS §1 says top-left is the number, never a filter: the screen §12 names as the reference was breaking rule 1 of the standard it is the reference for. THE SOURCE WAS FIXED FIRST, because a beautiful screen on a wrong number is worse than an ugly one. `api/dashboard/pipeline.mjs`'s `approval_amount_missing` over-reported three ways, and each one would have inflated the new headline. (1) AN EXCLUDED APPROVAL IS NOT WAITING — `applications.approval_excluded_at` (db/migrations/272) records, with a name and a time on it, that an approval does not count because it was withdrawn or never used; `client-control-panel.html`'s `isWaitingOnAmount` has always honoured that and this board did not, so the two screens disagreed about the same client and the board kept chasing a question somebody had already answered. (2) THE ROUND — the EXISTS matched every application the client had ever had, so one blank amount left behind in round 1 kept flagging through rounds 2 and 3 forever; it is now scoped to the client's newest round. (3) THE RAIL — one CARDS_SQL serves all eight boards, so the same client's Sales card and Inquiry Removal card carried a funding chip nobody looking at those boards can act on; the flag is now computed only on `funding_card_stacking` and `funding_altfin`. All three were proven by reverting the handler and watching the new database test fail, then restoring it and watching it pass. WHAT IS ON SCREEN: a headline row between the rail switcher and the filter bar, hard left — "WAITING ON US" as an eyebrow and the count at metric size, then ONE line naming ONE card ("Longest wait: Sam Turner — 10d in stage, New Lead") which scrolls to that card and rings it. "US", NOT "ME": `cards.owner` is never written by production code (src/workflows/cards.mjs:106, api/public/partner-apply.mjs:95 — only demo seeds set it), so every real card reads "unassigned" and a per-person number would be invented. To the right, three counters carrying WORDS — "on a bank", "on the client", "nothing recorded" — each of which filters the board when clicked, and the existing card/money/held figures move up beside them. EXACTLY ONE BUCKET PER CARD: a card can be in Round Submitted AND have a text nobody answered, so "us" wins first and us + bank + client + nothing recorded always equals the card count — if they double-counted the screen would contradict itself on its own first row. THE RULE IS DELIBERATELY NARROW. "Us" is an unanswered inbound message (a real EXISTS over `messages`) or an approval with no amount. "A bank" is the `round_submitted` stage and "the client" is `action_required`, THOSE TWO KEYS AND NO OTHERS — every other stage on every rail answers "nothing recorded", which is stated on screen as exactly that and never as "nothing to do". Guessing a party from a stage name nobody agreed on is the kind of number that looks real and is not. A DASH IS NOT A ZERO: before a rail answers and after a rail fails to answer, every figure is "—" and the line underneath says the number is unknown, because a 0 would tell an advisor nothing needs them, which is the one thing an unanswered read can never prove. ALSO: "Waiting on" joins the filter panel as a fifth select (including "Us — an approval amount", so the flag that has been on every card since 2026-08-29 is finally filterable), Sort gains "Amount needed first", and the age filter now reads the raw `entered_at` off the card instead of re-parsing the ROUNDED words it had already printed — "over 3 days" was being decided by a number rounded to the nearest hour or day before anything compared it. REFUSED, ON PURPOSE, EACH WITH A REASON: the dead "Held only" filter was left alone (wiring it needs `round_hold_reason`, which is funding-tier data behind Gate B, and removing it is the owner's call — either way it is a decision, not a detail); `read-signals.mjs`'s hardcoded `ready` and `total_approved` nulls stay honest blanks (a SUM over a table with no rows is 0, and 0 there would read as "no bank has ever approved anything"); and no "gone quiet" counter was built, because the endpoint returns two booleans and no last-message timestamp, so "quiet for 9 days" cannot be said today. UI-STANDARDS §12.6 WAS CORRECTED IN THE SAME CHANGE: both examples it gave of "status is never colour alone" — `.card.held` and `.hold-badge` — are real CSS that has never once painted, because nothing adds either class; the section now cites the live headline counters and `.c-needs-amount`, and says outright that an example which has never run cannot show that it works. NO NEW PAGE, SCREEN, TAB, MENU ROW, ROUTE, ENDPOINT OR MIGRATION. No font size is written on this screen — the metric is `.big` and the label is `.eyebrow`, so `fundhub-brand.css:189-204` hands both their sizes for free and pipeline.html still spends no escape hatch. `--fh-maxw` untouched, no width cap added, no empty-state wording changed. MEASURED on macOS, worktree `wf_6d2ee4ef-d09-7`, branch `feat/pipeline-waiting-on-triage` off main at 6e14b85c. Lint clean, 1611 files. It is worth knowing that this is the ordinary way a case becomes Escalated here (src/inquiry-removal/cases.mjs turns a failed call into Escalated, and by then the call has fired), so in practice the new sentence covers Blocked and little else. The behaviour is unchanged; only the claim about it is corrected. CORRECTION TO PR #315, MADE ON THE SAME BRANCH. That PR fixed a bug where a box the code had hidden was still on screen (`.round-amount{display:flex}` beats the browser's own `[hidden]{display:none}`, because an author `display` outranks the browser's default), then swept the screen for more and stopped two short. A verifier loaded the branch in a real browser and read the computed style; this correction was measured the same way. (1) THE ONE THAT MATTERS: `#ccp-consent-link` — the "Record consent for this client" link — could NEVER be hidden, because `.link-btn` is laid out with `display:flex`. FOUR branches of `checkConsent()` set `hidden = true` on it and all four were dead, so a 665x40 link sat on screen in every state carrying no information: consent already valid, consent REVOKED, the consent read failed, and an answer that came back with no status at all. The revoked one is the reason this carries the compliance label — the code's own comment beside that line reads "Revoked means stop, so do not offer a shortcut to re-collect it", and the screen offered the shortcut anyway. Fixed with `.link-btn[hidden]{display:none;}`. 2026-08-30 | role-funding-advisor (no journey file changed — the generator reads routes and gates, and this adds neither) | A ROUND CAN NO LONGER BE MARKED FUNDED WHILE A BANK APPROVAL ON IT HAS NO DOLLAR AMOUNT. Since 2026-08-30 (PR #304) the success fee is a percent of CONFIRMED APPROVALS — Approved applications carrying a recorded amount (docs/CLOSEOUT-FEE-BASIS.md) — so an approval nobody typed a number into is worth nothing on the bill, and once the round closed nobody ever went back for it. That was pure revenue leakage: the screen already warned "1 bank approval is still waiting on its dollar amount" but nothing stopped the round closing anyway. `guardFundedAmount` (src/funding/card-stacking-rounds.mjs), which already refused a funded round with no money at all, now refuses FIRST with `approval_amounts_missing` and NAMES THE BANKS still blank, so the advisor knows exactly what to go and fill in. Server-side only — the board's drag already calls it — and sending a funded amount does not override it, because the blank approval is the problem and a funded amount does not answer it. THE WAY OUT, AND WHY IT IS A FLAG AND NOT A STATUS: a bank can approve and the client never use it, or an approval can be withdrawn, so a hard block with no escape would deadlock a real round on one dead row and turn a revenue guard into a support ticket. New columns `applications.approval_excluded_at / _by / _reason` (db/migrations/272_approval_excluded.sql) record that an approval does not count, with WHO and WHEN, plus an `application_decisions` row exactly as every other decision writes one. `applications.status` is deliberately left at 'Approved': that column is what the BANK said, and src/plays/outcomes.mjs reads approval-vs-denial straight off it ('Approved' to yes, 'Denied' to no), so writing 'Denied' would invert the approval rate and inventing a third status would delete a real bank yes from that report. The audit row carries status NULL so an exclusion never shows up in the bank yes/no history as an outcome at all. An excluded approval also drops out of the fee basis even when it does carry an amount — an approval we are not billing for is not billed at any size. NULL still means unknown and still blocks (CLAUDE.md section 12); nothing defaults to 0, and an explicit zero is still refused as a denial rather than accepted as an approval. A round with no approvals behaves exactly as it did before, and an already-funded round is never re-blocked. Escape wired into the existing /api/applications (no new endpoint, no new screen) and onto the existing lender row in client-control-panel.html as a "Doesn't count" button that asks for a reason. 13 new tests in src/funding/approval-amount-guard.pg.test.mjs. | An approval left blank was money we never invoiced, and a guard with no way out would have deadlocked real rounds. | (this commit)

2026-08-30 | (no journey file changed — recorded deliberately, and the reason matters) | THE SCREEN STANDARD IS NOW WRITTEN DOWN, AND A TEST KEEPS IT TRUE. THE ROOT CAUSE: no settled standard for what a Fundhub screen looks like existed in writing anywhere an agent could see it. On 2026-08-19 two screens were reworked on the same day with OPPOSITE instructions and BOTH agents cited `docs/UI-STANDARDS.md` as their authority — neither was lying, because that document said nothing about what a container looks like at rest, what a topbar must carry, or that a font size written in a screen's own `<style>` is thrown away by the brand file. Each agent filled the gap with a reasonable guess and the guesses did not match. Worse, `docs/UI-STANDARDS.md` was reachable ONLY through a Cursor rule set to `alwaysApply:false`, which Claude Code never loads, so the document an agent was supposed to follow was invisible to it. THREE THINGS, and the smallest is the most important. (1) `docs/UI-STANDARDS.md` gains **§12 THE SCREEN FRAME**, written from what `public/app/pipeline.html` actually does (owner-set: pipeline is the reference for good): the resting container is `#fff` + `1px solid var(--line)` + a radius + `box-shadow:var(--panel-shadow)`; the shadow is NEVER written on a screen because `fundhub-brand.css:130-137` already gives it app-wide to `.card .stat .panel .tabs .editor .fos-panel .kpi-tile .big-tile .calc-panel .compose .ctx-card`, so a new container takes one of those names or joins that list; every OTHER shadow in pipeline.html is an interaction state, not decoration (hover :242, drop target :225, drag ghost :308-309, menu :315-316) — resting elevation is the token and anything stronger is an event; `border:0` on a shadow-list class is forbidden because it paints a floating shadow with no edge; a box that HOLDS boxes takes a flat tint and a hairline, never a shadow (`.col`, pipeline :215); status is never colour alone, because `paintBrand()` in `shell.js:2277-2286` overwrites `--alert/--warn/--ok/--info` from the tenant's own colour ramp and a single-hue white-label brand therefore paints every status identically — pipeline :243-244 survives that with a LEFT STRIPE and a tint, and :263-268 keeps literal amber with the reason written above the rule; the type trap and its ONE escape hatch per screen; and the topbar contract read off `client-control-panel.html:64-84`, including the note that `partner-galaxy.html:79-85` sets `flex-wrap:wrap` AND `overflow:hidden` on the same bar, so nobody copies it. (2) ONE LINE IN `CLAUDE.md` §3 — "Touching anything under `public/app/`? `docs/UI-STANDARDS.md` is law. Read it first." — which is the highest-leverage edit here, because it is what makes the standard reachable at all. (3) NEW `src/ui/screen-standard.test.mjs`, modelled on `src/ui/shell-is-fluid.test.mjs` (same screen discovery, same hand-written CSS walker, same house rule that a failure message must TEACH): no px font-size without `!important` in a shell screen's `<style>`; no topbar that wraps and then clips or pins a height; no shadow-list class dropping its border; and `CLAUDE.md` must still name `docs/UI-STANDARDS.md`. **IT LANDS RED ON PURPOSE — 5 tests, 2 pass, 3 fail — and the failure list IS the burndown for the screens phase.** MEASURED, NOT REPEATED: 210 dead px font-sizes across 10 of 29 shell screens — calendar 58, my-numbers 30, contracts 28, partner-galaxy 28, sales-floor 27, galaxy 24, closer-dashboard 9, consent-capture 4, client-control-panel 1, pipeline 1. The brief's prior figures for my-numbers (31) and contracts (30) counted inline `style=` attributes as well as `<style>` blocks; both are dead, and the inline ones are counted separately (closer-dashboard 6, contracts 2, my-numbers 1) plus `font:` shorthand (consent-capture 21, inquiry-remover 7, pipeline 2). ONE OFFENDER THE BRIEF DID NOT NAME: `ops-admin.html:128` `.aff-body .card{border:0}` keeps the app-wide shadow with no edge, the same fault as `closer-dashboard.html:102` and `:111`. `.gitattributes` is new and carries `docs/journeys/CHANGELOG.md merge=union`, because every branch in this batch appends to this file and would otherwise collide on the first line — "keep every entry" is exactly Git's built-in union driver. NO SCREEN WAS EDITED, no page, screen, tab or menu row was added, no copy was reworded, `--fh-maxw` was not touched, and no migration was added. | Two agents redesigned two screens on the same day from the same rulebook and got opposite answers, because the rulebook did not say what a screen looks like | (this commit)

Since PR #304 the success fee is a percent of CONFIRMED approvals — approvals with a recorded amount — so a forgotten box is a bill that never goes out. `src/adapters/mailgun.mjs:96` has always run a dollar pattern over every inbound bank email; it used the result only to nudge classification confidence and discarded the figure. Two things upstream destroyed the evidence as well: the event carried no body text at all, and `src/handlers/comms.mjs` bound the SUBJECT LINE to both the `subject` column and the `body_preview` column of every `bank_inbox` row — so each row showed the same sentence twice, the paragraph stating the amount was gone forever, and because `body_preview` was always populated the screen's `body_preview || classification || received_at` fallback could never reach its second or third option. FIXED IN FOUR PLACES. (1) The adapter now carries the figures: `findDollarAmounts` gathers every DISTINCT dollar figure from the subject and body, as fixed 2dp dollar strings, cents read as whole numbers so $450.10 never becomes $450.09. (2) `bodyPreviewOf` stores a real preview — whitespace flattened, capped at 500 characters with the cut marked, NULL when the email has no text. The WHOLE body is deliberately still not stored: `src/http/router.mjs:110` records what an uncapped body did to a shared production Postgres, and the back half of a forwarded bank statement is account detail nobody asked us to keep. (3) `onMailResponse` writes the preview into `body_preview` instead of a second copy of the subject, and NULL when there is none. (4) `GET /api/read/bank-inbox` returns two named keys out of `raw` — `amount_candidates` and `amount_candidates_found` — and still never returns the `raw` column itself; the endpoint's staff role gate, org gate and client gate are unchanged. ON SCREEN: the figures appear as one-click buttons immediately beside the Approved $ box on each lender row of the client control panel, plus one sentence above the list saying which email they came from. THREE RULES, DELIBERATE. It is a SUGGESTION — clicking fills the box and saves nothing; only Bank yes writes, and only after FHMoneyInput.parseAmount accepts what is in the box. It NEVER PICKS — a bank email routinely states a credit limit, an APR, an annual fee and a minimum payment in one paragraph, so when there is more than one figure the screen says outright "we cannot tell which one is the approval" and offers all of them. And NO FIGURE MEANS NO SUGGESTION, never a zero (CLAUDE.md §12). A DENIED email is not scanned at all — the number in a denial letter is the limit being refused, the most dangerous figure in the mailbox — and the classifier itself is untouched: the negation work from PR #298 that makes "not approved" stay DENIED was not edited, and a test pins its decisions. The other 2 are in `src/affiliates/economics.pg.test.mjs`, which imports only db.mjs and economics.mjs, fails on its own with nothing else running, and cannot be reached by this change. Playwright 9 new browser tests pass, and funded-amount + proxy-apply + screens-smoke (54 tests) stay green. Three fixes in `public/app/inquiry-remover.html` (the Specialist desk — Inquiry queue and Repair desk on one page). (1) COLUMNS. `paintRow()` indexed `r.cells[3]` and `r.cells[4]` — leftovers from a five-column Work Queue that no longer exists — so pressing "Mark confirmed" put the attempt count in the EXPECTED column and the status pill in CALL STATE, while the real Attempts and Status columns never moved: the row read "Removed" in the wrong place and still read open in the right one. Now `COL_ATTEMPTS = 6, COL_STATUS = 7`, derived from and pinned against the table's own header, and the same stale pair is fixed in `rowState()`'s DOM fallback. The comment above `paintRow` claimed the cell held a "2d 14h" stuck age; that column and its sample rows were deleted long ago and the sentence was pointing the next reader at the wrong two cells. (2) LISTENERS. `loadRepairDesk()` runs again after every Send, Stage, Enroll and drawer Send, and it re-bound the letter drawer, the PULL modal, a `document` keydown and all five filter tiles on each pass without removing the old ones. Two handlers on one tile ran the toggle twice — the first set `aria-pressed="true"`, the second read that and turned the filter straight back off — so one Send killed the desk's only way to sort itself, and the Escape listener accumulated one copy per reload for the life of the page. The chrome and the state it touches now live in a `repairDesk` IIFE and are bound once; `loadRepairDesk` binds only to rows it has just created and is published as `window.loadRepairDesk`. (3) SOFT PULL. `runSoftPull()` fired three POSTs at `/api/finance/crs-pull` and discarded every answer (`.then(next).catch(next)`), closing the box and reporting success by saying nothing. It now goes through `FHData.write`, stops at the first failure, and says so in `FHData.explain`'s house wording — mirrored into the desk's own error line because explain()'s "sample"-tone cases paint nothing on the bottom strip. OWNER-SET DECISION, `inquiry_specialist` DOES NOT PULL CREDIT: a soft pull is a real paid three-bureau order and an erasure cannot be undone; this desk disputes what is already on a report rather than ordering one, and `role-inquiry-remover-intended.md` already lists Finance and privacy among the routes this role stays blocked from. So the role was NOT added to `CRS_PULL_ROLES`; instead "Soft pull" and "Clean personal info" no longer render for roles their endpoints refuse (docs/UI-STANDARDS.md §5 — no control renders for a role that lacks permission), and both still render and work for owner and admin. A TRIGGER, NOT APPLICATION CODE, AND THE REASON IS COUNTABLE: two production writers already move a card, in two different files, neither aware of the other — `moveCardToStage` (`src/workflows/cards.mjs:98`) and `placeWhiteLabelRailCard` (`api/public/partner-apply.mjs:87`) — and a third path moves them in raw SQL, `161_optimization_repair_pipeline.sql` having bulk-repointed every optimization card at a new stage. ONLY ON A REAL CHANGE: the `WHEN` clause gates on `IS DISTINCT FROM`, so re-writing a card's CURRENT stage does not reset the clock — and that matters, because both writers are find-or-create and documented as safe to run twice, so a retried webhook would otherwise have made a stalled card look fresh every time it fired. A second half of the same clause skips the trigger when the statement also set `entered_at` itself, so a fixture can still backdate a card on purpose rather than waiting four days for a genuinely stale one. `kpis.mjs` SQL IS UNCHANGED and only its comment moved: the query always asked the right question and the column can finally answer it; a newly created card still counts, correctly, because it did just enter its first stage. Backfilling from it would stamp "moved recently" onto cards that were merely touched, which is this exact bug pointed the other way and strictly more dangerous, because it HIDES a quiet round instead of over-reporting one. There is no card-history table, no audit row, and `events` holds `round.*` payloads for one pipeline only, keyed by client, naming no card and no stage. So existing rows keep their creation time, read too STALE rather than too fresh, and become correct forever on their next real move.
