2026-08-29 | role-funding-advisor (no diagram changed — the classifier is a value on an existing step, not a new step) | A REJECTION EMAIL FROM A BANK WAS BEING READ AS AN APPROVAL. `src/adapters/mailgun.mjs` matched its keywords with a plain substring test (`lower.includes(kw)`), and the string "not approved" contains the string "approved". APPROVED is the first rule in the list and the first match won, so "Unfortunately, you were not approved for the requested credit limit" classified as APPROVED. That label is not decoration: `src/workflows/f-11-bank-email-event-router.mjs` reads it, and on APPROVED it moves the client's funding card into the `approved` stage of `funding_card_stacking`, sets `employee_next_action` to "Prepare Next Funding Round", and files the task "APPROVAL: log amount + update round". So a client who had just been turned down was recorded as approved, staff were told to prepare the next round on money that did not exist, and the denial follow-up (F-09) never ran because the event never said DENIED. Three more phrasings hit the same fault — "your request was not approved", "we have not approved your application", and any denial whose "not approved" fell across a line break — and the same substring test read "funded" out of "refunded" and "approved" out of "unapproved", so a fee refund also classified as APPROVED. FIXED THREE WAYS, all in the classifier, nothing downstream touched. (1) Keywords now match on WORD BOUNDARIES, and a space inside a keyword matches any run of whitespace so a hard-wrapped denial still matches. (2) NEGATION IS READ BEFORE THE KEYWORD CONTEST: "not approved", "unable to approve", "cannot approve", "will not be approved", "could not fund" and the like drop the APPROVED rule outright and record DENIED — because the leftover APPROVED keywords in a denial letter ("credit limit", "approval") are the letter describing what it is refusing. The gap between the negative and the approval word is a closed list of function words, not a wildcard, so "this does not affect your approved limit" and a "do not reply" footer stay APPROVED. (3) A shorter phrase sitting inside a longer phrase from another rule is discarded as the same words read twice. RULE ORDER WAS DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED — putting DENIED first only swaps which case breaks, and outcome-beats-process is what the order encodes: an approval that also says "verify your identity" or "please upload your ID" is still an approval, and those are pinned. OWNER-VISIBLE JUDGEMENT CALL: "We approved you for a reduced amount of $2,000" is now COUNTEROFFER, not APPROVED. Both move the card to the same stage, so the stage is not what is at stake — the task is. APPROVED files "log amount + update round", which reads as done; COUNTEROFFER files "review + log offer + next step", which puts a person on the shortfall. A cut-down approval needs the second one. A reduced RATE ("your APR has been reduced") is still APPROVED — it has to be a reduced amount. `classifyBankEmail` and `classifyFull` held two byte-identical copies of this loop; they are now one function with two entry points, so the fix cannot be applied to one and missed on the other. MEASURED: 9 of the assertions in the new tests FAIL on main and PASS on this branch, proven by running both classifiers side by side. 24 new tests, every phrasing one banks actually send. Unit suite 7161 tests / 7151 pass / 7 fail / 3 skipped on this branch vs 7137 / 7127 / 7 fail / 3 skipped on clean main at 4f551ff1 — THE SAME 7 FAILURES BY NAME, none new. The 131 database test files did not run in either measurement: `scripts/run-suite.mjs` exits after the unit phase when it is red, and it is red on main. Run on their own with DATABASE_URL unset they are 675 tests / 57 pass / 0 fail / 618 skipped. macOS, worktree agent-aff7ff77095f78d1a, no database connected. Lint clean. OWNER-SET 2026-08-28: auto-advance STAYS for R1→R2 and R2→R3 — those produce another bureau letter, a wrong move costs a letter and is recoverable, and the speed is worth it — and crossing INTO the escalation rounds requires a human confirmation, because a wrong move there produces sworn complaints, which is not the same class of mistake. The auto path passes `null` or the sentinel `"system_high_confidence"`, and NEITHER BUYS AN ESCALATION — pinned, including through the staff confirm path. `humanConfirmed` must be the literal `true`; `"true"`, `1`, `{}` and every other truthy value are refused. It defaults false, so a caller that says nothing gets the safe answer. THE HELD PARSE GOES TO THE EXISTING QUEUE, AND A REAL BUG WAS FOUND GETTING IT THERE. `confirmParse` now returns `{ok:false, reason:"escalation_needs_human", hold:true}` in exactly the shape the low-confidence hold already used, so `runParseAdvanceLoop`'s existing hold path stores it unconfirmed with no new branch. But `api/repair/exceptions.mjs` — the Inquiry Remover's exceptions screen — listed only parses with `confidence < 0.85`, and an escalation hold is a CONFIDENT read, so it would have sat in the table where NOBODY COULD EVER SEE IT. The parse is now stamped `heldForEscalation` in the existing `parse_json` column (no migration, no new table, no new queue) and that one query was widened to show both kinds. NO NEW QUEUE, NO NEW SCREEN, NO NEW ROUTE. PROVEN BOTH WAYS by 10 new tests in `src/metro2/rounds/rounds.test.mjs`, 5 in `src/repair/response-agent.test.mjs` and 3 in `src/underwrite/prior-outcome.test.mjs`: a confident machine read of an R3 verify is HELD, writes no item row, and does NOT release the complaints; the same answer confirmed by a staff id reaches R4 and does release them; a held item is still reported as needing escalation so nothing is dropped; the round cap still wins over the human gate; and — the regression most likely to break by accident — R1→R2 and R2→R3 still advance with nobody in the loop, pinned at both the state-machine level and end to end through `runParseAdvanceLoop`. | Owner: auto-advance is fine up the bureau ladder, but a machine must not decide that somebody swears a federal complaint | (this commit)

THE RULE DID NOT CHANGE, THE SOURCE OF TRUTH DID: **a claim must be backed by a row, not by an expectation.** NOTHING NEW WAS BUILT TO SEND OR TO STORE. `dispute_letters` (`db/migrations/160_metro2_dispute_engine.sql`) already carries `round` as free text so R4 and R5 already fit, already flips to `status='sent'` when the mail provider accepts a letter (`src/repair/send.mjs`), and `src/metro2/rounds/store.mjs saveLetter` already takes a `target`. NEW `src/metro2/rounds/complaint-filing.mjs` holds the two pieces that genuinely did not exist: WHERE a complaint is mailed, and HOW the record is read back. `loadComplaintFilings` is read-only and filters in SQL on `target IN ('cfpb','state_ag')` AND `status IN ('sent','delivered')` AND the round matching the target, so a row that comes back is by construction a complaint a mail provider accepted. A GENERATED COMPLAINT IS NOT A FILED ONE — `'generated'`, `'ready'`, `'variance_failed'` and `'failed'` all count as not mailed. A corrupt row whose round and target disagree is discarded, not trusted. A database failure returns ZERO filings and is reported, never thrown, so a hiccup makes Round 6 say LESS, never more. `src/metro2/letters/generate.mjs buildLetterText` gained one optional `priorFilings` input, rendered through the same evidence-block mechanism the existing PRIOR BUREAU RESPONSE facts already use — no new letter shape — as `COMPLAINTS ALREADY FILED (evidence):` with one plain sentence per recorded mailing. It is gated to R6 alone, and to a non-empty record: with no record the R6 letter renders EXACTLY as it did before this path existed. `src/repair/analyze.mjs` loads the record only when it is building an R6 letter. So `agPostalAddress()` returns null for every state, `complaintDestination` refuses that send with `ag_postal_address_unknown`, `sendRepairLetters` stops before mailing so NO filing row is written, and Round 6 stays silent about a state AG filing exactly as it does today. It is labelled "Final notice, reissued" so it never prints Round 3's title or Round 3's timing. **R6 DOES NOT AND MAY NOT SAY THE COMPLAINTS WERE FILED**, and that is a FINDING, not a gap that was filled: NOTHING IN THIS REPOSITORY RECORDS THAT A CLIENT EVER FILED ONE. Writing "I have filed a CFPB complaint" into R6 would be the identical defect already fixed on main, where the Round 3 letter told the bureau a CFPB complaint was being filed and no such document had ever been produced. Pinned: no R4–R6 opening, closing, instruction or rendered letter claims a filing happened — every CFPB and attorney-general reference in them is future tense. That sworn sentence was false and the federal complaint was out of order. A CLIENT CAN NEVER REACH R4 BY DEFAULT, BY GUESS OR BY EMPTY DATA: an empty list, a missing argument, a null round, "R9", the number 4, "round 4" and a database error all refuse, and a database failure WITHHOLDS the complaints rather than releasing them. 2026-08-28 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | **COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED** — dispute logic. UnderwriteIQ could only ever write a Round 1 dispute letter. Rounds 2 and 3 were gated on one field on each account, `priorOutcome`, which `src/underwrite/letter-pack.mjs` read and **nothing in this repository ever wrote**. The vendor letter writer (`vendor/underwriteiq-full/api/lite/letter-generator.js`, `getAccountsForRound`) hands out the Round 2 letter only to accounts whose `priorOutcome` is exactly `"verified"` and the Round 3 letter only to `"verified_round2"`, so both matched zero accounts every time. That is why the escalation ladder's top rung was out of reach: the CFPB and state attorney general complaints ship inside `06-complaints-CONDITIONAL/` behind a cover sheet reading DO NOT FILE WITH ROUND 1 — send only after Round 3 is done, and Round 3 could not be reached. NOTHING NEW WAS BUILT TO CAPTURE A BUREAU ANSWER, because a capture path already existed and works: staff post a scanned bureau letter to `/api/repair/inbound-mail`, `src/metro2/inbound/parse-response.mjs` reads it, a human confirms it (`src/metro2/inbound/confirm.mjs`, or `/api/repair/exceptions` when confidence is low), `src/metro2/rounds/state.mjs applyItemOutcome` moves the account up a round, and `src/repair/parse-loop.mjs persistAdvancedItems` writes the answer to `dispute_items`. What was missing was one wire from that stored answer to the letter pack's gate. New file `src/underwrite/prior-outcome.mjs` reads it — read-only, never writes — and `buildLetterPackForClient` now makes a third read for it. THE GATE: an account advances only when all three of `status = 'escalated'`, `outcome = 'verified'` and `round = R2..R6` are true in the database at once, which only `applyItemOutcome` can produce and only from a confirmed answer. An unanswered account is `open` or `sent` and never advances; an account that hit the program's round cap is `closed` and never advances; a deleted, updated or unaddressed account is finished and never advances. Matching is strict on purpose — same bureau, then the last four digits of the account number; a creditor name alone is accepted only when the stored record carries no account number AND exactly one account at that bureau bears that name, so two cards from one bank can never both escalate off one answer. Rounds 4–6 reuse the Round 2 / Round 3 letter shapes through the repository's existing `promptPoolRound` rather than a second rule being invented. Proven by `src/underwrite/prior-outcome.test.mjs` (18 tests): with no recorded answer the pack still writes Round 1 and only Round 1 — that regression is pinned first — and with a recorded answer real `ex_round2.pdf` and `ex_round3.pdf` files come out. WHAT A HUMAN STILL DOES BY HAND: nothing in this change mails a bureau letter or reads a bureau reply. Someone still has to put the bureau's scanned reply into `/api/repair/inbound-mail`, and confirm it if the reader is unsure. LEFT AS IT WAS, NOT FIXED HERE: two accounts pre-date this work — the Round 3 letter text still tells the bureau a CFPB complaint is being filed, and the complaint pair is still gated on the pack containing at least one dispute letter rather than on Round 3 having actually been reached. NOT REGENERATED, AND WHY: `docs/journeys` is stale by the same 9 files before and after this change; the journey extractor reads only `netlify/functions/api.mjs`, `src/http/read-api.mjs`, `src/http/partner-read-api.mjs` and `src/http/dashboard-auth.mjs`, this change touches none of them and adds no route, so regenerating here would sweep in another change's undocumented `/api/public/optimize` routes. | Rounds 2 and 3 were dead code the owner had paid for, and the escalation ladder's last rung could not be reached in normal use | (this commit)

Cause, recorded so it is not repeated: a changelog append written as `open(p,'w').write(new + open(p).read())` — `open(p,'w')` truncates the file BEFORE the read runs, so the read returns empty and only the new line survives. Read first, then open for write. | Self-inflicted, caught by an adversarial review before merge | (this commit)

2026-08-27 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | Kills the last sideways page scroll in the CRM and makes Pipeline's board fit the owner's 2560 monitor. TWO SCREENS, ONE BUG: campaign-manager (381px of sideways scroll at 1440) and social-studio (195px) were not two problems. shell.js mountChip builds the session chip with its geometry as an INLINE style including flex-shrink:0, measured 558px wide, and placeInHeader drops it into whatever top bar the screen already has. In a bar that is already full, an unshrinkable 558px child pushes the whole PAGE sideways. The four declarations that let it shrink already existed but sat inside the 860px phone block, so they never applied on a desktop; nothing about the problem is phone-shaped, so they are hoisted to apply at every width. That alone also fixed creative-factory, which was scrolling 144px at 1280 and had not been reported. SHRINKING ALONE WAS NOT ENOUGH, and the second half matters: at 1280 the chip obediently collapsed to 26px while its own children kept their intrinsic widths, so a 106px label still hung 101px off the page AND the chip had become unreadable. Squeezing harder makes both worse. So a bar that holds the chip may now WRAP, letting the chip drop to its own row at a readable width. Scoped with :has() to bars that actually contain it, and covering BOTH containers shell.js can choose — `.topbar` and `.topbar-right` (11 screens have the latter; they measure clean today, and covering only the broken one is the half-fix that leaves the other as a landmine). PIPELINE AT 2560: ten columns at the 238px minimum need 2498px and only 2332px exists, so the board scrolled 166px on the owner's monitor at 100% zoom. From 2560 up the minimum may relax to 216px, and flex-grow then pushes the columns back out to fill what is actually there — 220px at 2560, 308px at 3440. Deliberately NOT lowered below 2560: there the board scrolls either way, so narrowing the cards would cost laptop users width and buy nothing. Verified live on all 29 shell screens at 1280 / 1440 / 2560 / 3440: 29/29 clean at every width, zero page-level sideways scroll anywhere, and pipeline board scroll 0 at both 2560 and 3440. Before this change: 26/29 at 1280 and 27/29 at 1440. | Owner-set 2026-08-27: every tab must look the same and none of them may scroll sideways | (this commit)
2026-08-27 | role-sales-manager, role-closer (no route/gate change) | Pipeline's board columns now STRETCH to fill a wide window instead of stopping short of the right edge (owner-set). Was `.col{width:238px;flex-shrink:0}`, which pinned the board at 2498px however wide the monitor got — measured 714px of empty board to the right of the last column at 3440. Now `flex:1 1 238px;min-width:238px`: columns share the free space when there is any and never shrink below the width a card was designed for. `width:max-content` REMOVED from `.board` and it must not come back — with a growable column it stops meaning 'as wide as the columns', because intrinsic sizing resolves each item at its CONTENT max-content; tried first and measured a 3501px board with 338px columns at every viewport, scrolling even at 3440. The board now takes its normal auto width (the scroll wrap's), which is what gives the columns space to grow into. A 4px `::after` spacer restores the trailing gap after the last column that the old max-content used to give via padding. Measured live at four widths: 3440 board 3212 / columns 308 / no scroll and no empty board; 2560 columns 238 and 166px of board scroll; 1440 columns 238 and 1286px of board scroll; 1280 columns 238 and 1446px — the last three identical to before, so nothing regressed on a narrower screen. Page-level sideways scroll stayed 0 at every width. NOTE for the owner: at 2560 at 100% zoom ten 238px columns need 2498px and only 2332px exists, so the board still scrolls 166px there — exactly as it did before. It fills once the window is wider than that, which is what zooming out does. The 238px minimum was NOT lowered to force a fit, because that is a card-design decision, not a layout one. | Owner-set 2026-08-27: the page was fluid but the board was not, so Pipeline alone looked unfinished on a wide monitor | (this commit)
2026-08-27 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | Locks the fluid layout in so it cannot drift back. New guard `src/ui/shell-is-fluid.test.mjs` (7 tests) fails if any screen re-caps its own page column, if the crm-sidebar.css shell rule stops saying `max-width:none !important`, if `.fh-maxw` regains a cap, if the `--fh-maxw` token is deleted (client-portal.html reads it for its 780px reading column), or if the 860px phone pin is removed. The guard immediately caught two real landmines and they are removed in the same commit: `my-numbers.html` `.shell{max-width:1240px;margin:0 auto}` and `sales-floor.html` `.shell{max-width:1280px;margin:0 auto}`. Both were already DEAD — crm-sidebar.css overrode them with !important — but anyone deleting the shell rule believing it to be 'the cap' would have got those two numbers back and left those screens narrow while every other tab filled the monitor. Audited all 36 pages in public/app: 29 carry the shell and every one is fluid; of the remaining 7, only `client-portal.html` is referenced by the staff nav and its 780px reading column is deliberate. `soft-pull-approve.html` keeps its 560px form width — a client approval form, not a tab. Verified on production after the merge of #248: all five sampled screens 0 dead margin at 1440/2560/3440, and every major layout block on all 29 screens grows when the window widens. KNOWN AND NOT CHANGED: the Pipeline board's columns are a fixed 238px each (10 columns = 2498px), so on a 3440 window the board stops 714px short of the right edge — the page is fluid, the board's columns are not. That is a design decision for the owner, not a bug in the shell. | Owner-set 2026-08-27: every tab must look the same when switching between them, and must stay that way without anyone policing it by hand | (this commit)
2026-08-27 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | Follow-on to the full-width change, same day. THREE screens mount the shell's account row (Journeys, My Numbers, Sales Floor) and on Journeys it was stealing 718px of the window. Root cause in shell.js placeInHeader: the host was picked with one grouped `querySelector(".shell, .main, .content, main, .app-shell, .app")`, and a selector list returns the first match in DOCUMENT order, not list order. `.app` is an ANCESTOR of `.shell`, so the grouped form always resolved to `.app` and never to the content column it was written to prefer. Harmless while `.app` is display:block (My Numbers and Sales Floor just get a full-width block row), but Journeys sets `.app{display:flex}`, so the row became a flex ITEM sitting between the rail and the content. Measured live: Journeys painted its content in a 494px column at 1440 and left a 1065px gap at 3440. Fixed by trying the hosts in order, innermost first. After: all three screens 0 gap on both sides, content 1212px at 1440 and 3212px at 3440, no sideways scroll. Also in this change, unrelated to layout: tsconfig.json ADDED — CLAUDE.md §6 requires `npx tsc --noEmit` to pass and there was no tsconfig at all, so tsc found no inputs, printed its help and exited 0; the gate had been passing by checking nothing for its whole life. checkJs is off on purpose (measured 5393 errors if enabled wholesale), files opt in with `// @ts-check`, and money.mjs and chunk.mjs are opted in as the first two. CLAUDE.md §12 updated with the first real database-suite measurement: 6867 tests, 6866 pass, 0 skipped, and the one failure is a connection-role artifact that passes as fundhub_app. | Journeys was unusable wide — content in a 494px column — and two 'done' gates were not checking anything | (this commit)
2026-08-27 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | CRM content now fills the browser window at any zoom instead of stopping at 1800px and centring. Three rules applied the same cap and all three had to go: `.fh-maxw` in fundhub-brand.css (max-width and the auto inline margin dropped, width:100% kept), the shell rule at crm-sidebar.css that made the cap global on 2026-08-18 (inverted to `max-width:none !important` rather than deleted — deleting it would have handed sales-floor.html its own hardcoded 1280px and my-numbers.html its 1240px back, leaving those two narrow while every other screen filled the monitor), and `#fosWrap` in finance-os.html, an ID selector the shell rule cannot reach, which would otherwise have been the single screen still capped. margin-inline is pinned to 0 rather than removed because those same screens set `margin:0 auto` and an auto cross-axis margin cancels flex stretch, which shrink-wraps a wrapper to its widest child. The `--fh-maxw` token is deliberately KEPT at 1800px though nothing caps with it any more: client-portal.html reads it as `min(780px, var(--fh-maxw))` to hold the client's 780px reading column, and deleting the token makes that min() invalid, which drops max-width to none and blows the client portal full width. The phone pin (`max-width:100%` under @media max-width:860px, UI-STANDARDS §11 no sideways scroll) and the 228px rail offset (`padding-left:var(--fh-side-w)`) are both untouched. docs/UI-STANDARDS.md §1 updated in the same commit: "Max content width 1800px, centered" is replaced by "Content fills the window. No max width.", keeping the note on why 1280 was abandoned. | Owner-set 2026-08-27: zooming out on a 2560px monitor to get more room returned none — content stopped growing and every extra pixel became empty side margin (532px dead at 2560, 1412px at 3440). Raising the cap 1280->1800 on 2026-08-17 had not fixed that; removing it does. | (this commit)
2026-08-27 | role-funding-advisor, role-owner (no route/gate change) | The Fulfillment "Needs Pull" and "Needs Consent" tiles were silently dropping almost every client. The fraud check compared TRIM(custom_fields->>'round_hold_reason') to 'Fraud Alert' with no COALESCE, so a client with no hold reason - the common case - produced NULL, which poisoned the whole OR, made NOT NULL null, and COUNT(*) FILTER skipped the row. Both tiles read 0 with a client that qualified on every other term. Adding COALESCE fixes it; a test now pins that a qualifying client must land in one of the two counts. | Owner: Needs Pull tile undercounted | (this commit)

2026-08-19 | role-owner, role-sales-manager, role-closer, role-funding-advisor, role-inquiry-remover (no route, no gate, and no `-actual.md` moved) | **COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED** — four numbers on the Fulfillment lens were telling lies, found by an adversary pass over the entries below. Same two screens, nothing else touched. **1. The tile disagreed with the row beneath it.** The "Needs Pull" tile counted a client as waiting on a credit pull whenever they had paid and had no credit report row on file. The chip on the row used one more test that the tile did not: a client whose credit status is already recorded as Complete is not waiting on anything. Measured on real Postgres, the tile said 4 while only 2 of those clients got the chip. The tile now uses the same rule as the chip, and the "waiting on written permission" sentence under it uses it too, so no client falls out of both counts. **2. Demo rows counted as real ones everywhere except credit.** Practice data is marked with a demo flag. The credit read always skipped it; the other reads did not. So with Demo Mode OFF, a practice task added a real client to "Action Needed" and painted a warning pill on their row, and a practice funding round could put a funding step on their file. Every read that can skip practice rows now does — tasks, funding rounds, funding cards, documents and inquiry cases. Two cannot: the dispute tables have no demo flag, and the invoice balance view does not carry the one on invoices; both would need a migration and this layer writes no schema, so both are recorded in the code rather than left silent. **Trade-off, recorded not hidden:** with Demo Mode ON, a practice client's own practice rows are now skipped too, so their file shows fewer warnings than before. That is the same behaviour the credit read has always had. **3. A failed read could read as "nothing is wrong with this file".** The warning list is built from three reads. If any one of them refused, the list came back empty and nothing was marked as unread — and an empty warning list is the whole test for "Ready to Fund". So an unreachable table could turn into a step saying the file is clear to fund. A read that fails now says so, that one step answers "we do not know", and the screen falls back to today's display. It does not take away an answer that never needed the list: a client whose step is "Get Consent" still gets it, and a repair-only client still correctly shows no funding step. **4. A tile counting everybody sat over a list that stops short.** "Total clients" counts the whole book while the list below it asks for one page. At 47 clients they match; above the page size the screen made a claim it could not back up. The tile now carries one sentence — "The list below shows 200 of these 431." — and only when the two numbers really disagree. No seventh tile, no paging control. **Read and display only:** no write, no migration, no column, no route, no screen, no tab, no menu row, no workflow touched. **Proof:** every fix has a test that fails against the code as it was — checked by putting each defect back and watching the test fail. `src/fulfillment/read-signals.pg.test.mjs` gains a fixture with a practice task and practice funding round on a REAL client, and a third org proving the tile and the chip agree on "Complete"; `src/fulfillment/read-signals.test.mjs` gains four tests that force the warning read to refuse; `src/fulfillment/next-action.test.mjs` gains five more on the same rule; `src/http/pipeline-screen.test.mjs` gains four on the new sentence. No test weakened, none deleted. | A tile that disagrees with the row under it is worse than no tile, practice data was being counted as real with Demo Mode off, and a failed read was coming out as a clean bill of health. | uncommitted (worktree `feat/fulfillment-layer-phase1`)
2026-08-19 | role-owner, role-sales-manager, role-closer, role-funding-advisor, role-inquiry-remover (no route, no gate, and no `-actual.md` moved) | **COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED** — three display defects fixed on the same two screens as the two entries directly below, and nothing else touched. **1. The big Next Action line no longer goes blank.** When the system worked a file out and found no step that applies, the line went empty and the page painted a bare em dash — with the grey "Saved on the record: Pull CRS" line sitting right underneath it. A heading, a dash, then the forbidden words: it read as though the dash were hiding the real instruction. The line now reads "No step applies right now." All three answers are plain sentences and no two read the same: the step itself, "No step applies right now.", or "Not worked out yet." The saved value stays exactly where Chris approved it — a quiet line underneath, labelled as what is on the record, never as an instruction. **2. Four money displays that were already on the screen are back to what they printed on `main`.** Prequal, Total Approved and the two income-per-year lines had been switched onto the new shared money rule, which changed them: a recorded zero became "$0" where it had been a dash, and a negative became "$-500". Nobody asked for either. They are back on `main`'s rule byte for byte. The one shared whole-dollars rule stays for the two NEW amounts — this page's funding-round approved amount and the pipeline lens's — so the $50,000-reading-as-$500 defect the entry below fixed stays fixed. **Whether a recorded zero on those old tiles should read "$0" or a dash is Chris's call and was deliberately not made here**; it reads as a dash, which is what it read before this work. **3. Funding money could reach a client who only bought credit repair.** A `funding_rounds` row carries an approved amount, and both screens painted the newest one for anybody who had one, so a REPAIR_ONLY client could read "Round 1 · approved · approved $75,000". No chip is involved, so Gate B's chip guard never saw it. **Gate B now covers the money as well as the chip, through the same helper and the same whitelist** — `fundingChipsAllowed()` in `src/fulfillment/next-action.mjs`, reading `src/config/product-path.mjs`. A round comes back only on the three funding tiers; repair-only, an unrecognised tier and no tier at all get nothing, and that refusal does NOT mark the file as unread — it is the right answer, not a failure. The Client Control Panel's round block also stopped reading the raw newest row as a fallback, which was the way around the gate; it now shows the worked-out round or dashes, and the round number, status and hold reason are still on the page in the tiles that always showed them. **Read and display only:** no write, no migration, no column, no route, no screen, no tab, no menu row, no workflow touched. `npm run journeys` still generates every page byte-identical, the correct result for a change that adds no route and no gate. **Proof:** `src/fulfillment/next-action.test.mjs` gains five tests that fail if a repair-only client is handed a funding round, one of them asserting the round and the chip agree tier for tier so there can only ever be one whitelist; `src/http/client-panel-screen.test.mjs` gains three tests pinning the old tiles to `main`'s rule and three that fail if either screen reads a raw funding-round row again, and the one test that asserted the old blank slot now asserts the plain words. No test weakened, none deleted. Both screens were also loaded in a real browser: a repair-only client carrying a $75,000 round row shows dashes in the round block and no round line on the pipeline lens, while a full-funding client still shows Round 1 · approved · $75,000. | A bare dash above the words "Pull CRS" is the worst way that line could read, tiles nobody asked to change had changed, and funding money was reaching people who never bought funding. | uncommitted (worktree `feat/fulfillment-layer-phase1`)
2026-08-19 | role-owner, role-sales-manager, role-closer, role-funding-advisor, role-inquiry-remover (no route, no gate, and no `-actual.md` moved) | **COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED** — fixes two defects an adversary pass found in the Fulfillment Layer Phase 1 entry directly below, both on the same two screens and nowhere else. **1. The big Next Action line no longer shows the value saved on the record.** It used to, whenever the answer could not be worked out — and that is exactly the case where consent could not be established, while the saved value is written with no consent check at all and is the literal words "Pull CRS" in `src/workflows/s-06-post-call-funding-purchased.mjs` and `src/workflows/c-05-pre-funding-review.mjs`. A client with no recorded consent could therefore be shown "Pull CRS" on screen, which is the compliance gate Chris called non-negotiable. The big line now only ever shows a step the system worked out; when nothing was worked out it says "Not worked out yet." in plain words. **Nothing is lost:** the saved value moves to a quiet grey line underneath reading "Saved on the record: Pull CRS", so staff keep the information and simply stop being told to act on it. This corrects the sentence in the entry below that says the panel shows the value last saved on the record — it does not any more, and that was the leak. **2. The two screens agreed to disagree about money.** `public/app/pipeline.html` divided a number by a hundred and `public/app/client-control-panel.html` did not, so the same amount could print as $50,000 on one screen and $500 on the other. The real unit was traced, not guessed: `funding_rounds.approved_amount` is a `numeric(14,2)` column and reaches the browser as the text "50000.00", meaning fifty thousand dollars, and `custom_fields.total_funding_estimate` is saved in whole dollars as a plain number. **Both are dollars.** They are exceptions to the integer-cents rule in `CLAUDE.md` §12, which describes `src/commissions/money.mjs`; that module converts into cents at its own door (`src/commissions/calculate.mjs` calls `toCents()` on a funded amount), so cents never leave the commission maths. Both screens now use the one dollars rule, written in a comment beside each formatter. Nothing on file still prints as an em dash and never as $0; a zero that really is recorded prints as $0 on both, because hiding a recorded zero behind a dash calls a known fact unknown. **Read and display only, same as the entry below:** no write, no migration, no column, no route, no screen, no tab, no menu row, no workflow touched. Two screens and two test files, nothing else. `npm run journeys` still generates every page byte-identical, which is the correct result for a change that adds no route and no gate. **Proof:** `src/http/client-panel-screen.test.mjs` is new and fails if the saved text reaches the big line again or if the two screens ever format the same amount differently; `src/http/pipeline-screen.test.mjs` had its money tests corrected from cents to dollars, none removed or weakened. 2026-08-19 | role-owner, role-sales-manager, role-closer, role-funding-advisor, role-inquiry-remover (no route, no gate, and no `-actual.md` moved) | **COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED** — the Fulfillment Layer, Phase 1, built on the Phase 0 mapping at `docs/workflows/fulfillment-layer-2026-08-19.md` and Chris's option A. **Read and display only: no INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE anywhere, no migration, no new table, no new column, no new route, no new screen, no new tab, no new menu row, and no workflow or job touched.** **`npm run journeys` was re-run and every generated page came back byte-identical, which is the correct result** — these pages track routes and the gate on each one, and this work adds neither; it adds derived fields to two read endpoints that already existed and paints them on two screens that already existed. That is the same outcome the 2026-08-19 role-inquiry-remover entry below recorded for the same reason. **What a staff member now sees.** Chris's Employee Next Action field is blank on 39 of 47 client records, so the two screens stop showing that saved stamp on its own and instead work the answer out from the client's own records: `src/fulfillment/read-signals.mjs` gathers the evidence with SELECTs only and `src/fulfillment/next-action.mjs` decides, with no database access of its own so the same answer reaches every screen from one rule instead of three copies. The Client Control Panel (`public/app/client-control-panel.html`) gains one read-only block — next action with the sentence explaining why, the active blockers, and where the funding round stands with its finalized state — and its existing "Next action" line is now driven by the same worked-out answer so the two lines on one page cannot disagree. The pipeline board (`public/app/pipeline.html`) gains a **Board / Fulfillment switch on the filter bar it already had**; Board is the default and is byte-for-byte the screen that shipped before, and turning the lens on only sets `hidden` on the board row — no card, column, listener, filter or scroll position is rebuilt, re-queried or re-sorted, and turning it off restores every one of them, including the previous hidden state of the search box, the filter button, its chips and the summary. The lens lists the clients with a next-action chip on each row, the six rollup tiles, and click-a-chip-to-filter. **The order is Chris's, first match wins, and nothing in the code implies it** (Phase 0 §4 says so plainly): Clear Fraud Alert, Get Consent, Pull CRS, Remove Inquiries, Collect Documents, Review Disputes, Review Funding File, Prepare Next Round, Apply for Funding, Ready to Fund. "Lock Fee" is deliberately not a queue chip — money-side signal only — and "File Prep" is deliberately dropped. **TWO COMPLIANCE GATES, EACH BUILT TWICE.** GATE A: a client with no live written permission is never shown "Pull CRS"; their step is "Get Consent", which is ranked above the pull so first-match-wins refuses it, AND `guardConsentBeforePull()` refuses it again on the final answer while knowing nothing about the order, so reordering the list cannot defeat it. This closes Phase 0 defect 2, where the pull button was correctly greyed out but the words above it were not checked at all, so staff read an instruction the system would then refuse — the screen and the button now read the same consent through the same `consentStatus()` call `src/finance/soft-pulls.mjs` gates the pull on, and `src/consent/index.mjs` exports its validity predicate (additive, behaviour unchanged) so a 500-client list cannot fork the rule into a second hand-typed copy. GATE B: a repair-only client is never shown a funding chip — Review Funding File, Prepare Next Round, Apply for Funding, Ready to Fund — enforced by the chip's own predicate AND again by `guardFundingProduct()`, which reads its own separately written list of the four and fails closed on an unknown, missing or unrecognised product type. That closes Phase 0 defect 1 for the display, where a repair-only client is being told to "Apply for Funding" today. Both gates, both halves, have tests that fail if the guard is removed (`src/fulfillment/next-action.test.mjs`, 78 tests). **When a signal cannot be read the screen falls back to today's display — never a blank, never a crash, never a guess.** Every derivation is wrapped; the four new fields are absent from the reply rather than blank; an unknown answer sets `next_action_degraded` and the panel says in words that this is the value last saved on the record. Blank survives as blank: the derivation reads the raw `custom_fields` blob and the raw tag array rather than the response mapper, because that mapper turns a missing `crs_paid` into `false` (Phase 0 defect 7) and "we do not know" is not "no". Money is passed through exactly as Postgres hands it over and a NULL approved amount stays a dash — never $0. **Two of the six tiles are `null` on purpose, and that is the answer, not a gap**: "Ready" and "Total Approved" have no honest source (Phase 0 §5), so each carries a sentence saying so rather than a zero, which would be a claim nobody can stand behind; "Total Prequal" is shown beside the number of clients it came from, because Phase 0 measured the whole company total coming from one client out of 47. **GAPS RECORDED, NOT RECONCILED, and no `-intended.md` was edited:** (1) **no `-intended.md` file names either screen at all** — the Client Control Panel and the pipeline board appear in none of the eight, only `role-inquiry-remover-intended.md` describes a screen and that screen is the Specialist desk — so nothing hand-written says what a next action should say, what order the chips belong in, or that a permission gate or a product-type gate should exist there; Chris's order and both gates are owner-set direction from this batch, not journey-derived, and an agent may not write them into the intended file; (2) **"Ready to Fund" is a step the code now has that no intended journey and no Phase 0 finding authorises** — Phase 0 row 10 recorded zero matches anywhere in the system for it; it was added by the build lead, ranked last, and given a derived meaning (nothing blocking, newest round not on hold, paperwork complete) that is a definition nobody wrote down, and it needs Chris to say whether that is what he means; (3) **the lens shows an eleventh chip that is not on Chris's list** — "Not enough information", the honest label for a client whose next step could not be worked out; it is clickable and filterable like the other ten, and Chris has not seen it; (4) **`role-sales-manager` and `role-inquiry-remover` can now be told on screen to "Get Consent" and have no route to do it** — both reach `/api/dashboard/client` and `/api/dashboard/clients` as staff, but both are blocked from `/api/consent/capture`, which admits owner, admin, closer and funding_advisor only; their own intended pages say that block is correct, so this is a real gap between what the screen asks of those two desks and what the system lets them do, and nothing was changed to close it; (5) **the funding chips fail closed on an unrecorded product type**, which is the safe posture `src/config/product-path.mjs` requires, but it means a client whose tier has never been written sees no funding chip at all — that is honest rather than wrong, and it will look like the funding chips are missing until `clients.outcome_tier` is filled in; (6) **these generated pages cannot show a screen change at all** — they record routes and gates, so a reader comparing intended against actual will see nothing of this work on either page, and this changelog line is the only record of it in `docs/journeys/`; (7) Phase 0's twelve live bugs are untouched and remain Chris's for a later batch — in particular `crs_count` on the client list still counts demo rows as real pulls, and the workflow at `src/workflows/c-03-inquiry-removed-resume-or-hold.mjs:45` still writes "Apply for Funding" onto a repair-only client's record without checking what they bought; Now an owner or admin can approve or refuse a held post from the waiting list on Social Studio, and approve or send back a submitted brand from the Creative Factory review queue. **APPROVE AND REFUSE ARE OWNER/ADMIN ONLY, enforced on the server, not by hiding a button** — a partner approving their own credit-repair post would defeat the entire hold. That tightening is the only journey delta: `/api/social/posts` moves from `partner, staff` to `employees: owner, admin` plus partner, matching the shape `/api/brand/review` already has. **Recorded fidelity loss:** the extractor reads a gate per endpoint, so the page now describes the whole of `/api/social/posts` as owner/admin-limited when in truth only approve and refuse are — list, queue and discard still serve a partner. Three defects found by adversarial review and fixed before merge, each PROVEN by a test that fails against the pre-fix code: (1) a refusal did not check whether it had actually changed a row, so a post moved by a concurrent approve could be reported refused while it stayed queued and still sent — the refuse arm now RETURNs and throws the same NOT_HELD the approve arm already threw, and because both statements share one transaction the mirror-row write rolls back with it; (2) `withPartnerScope({kind:'partner'})` scopes row-level security to the NAMED partner, not to the caller's company, so one company's owner could approve another company's held post — an org predicate copied verbatim from `api/brand/review.mjs` now guards it, applied ONLY to approve and refuse, and a post belonging to another company answers identically to one that does not exist so the endpoint cannot confirm it is there; (3) two screens still told the reader the opposite of what the code does — the approval panel said "nothing can approve one yet", and the copy-check preview a writer reads BEFORE saving said "needing approval does not hold it back… the post still goes out at its time", which had been false since T11 merged. Both rewritten. GAPS recorded, not reconciled: (1) **no `-intended.md` names an approval step**, so this is recorded as a gate change only and no `-intended.md` was edited; (2) **nothing records WHO refused a post** — there is no `rejected_by` column on either table and none was added; approving does record who and when, refusing is distinguishable from an automatic refusal only by the reason written onto the row; (3) **approve and refuse sit behind the existing "is this partner's marketing suite switched on" check**, so switching a partner's marketing off — the natural reaction to a marketing problem — locks their held posts in place permanently; that ordering is an owner decision and was deliberately not changed; (4) posts already handed to a social account had appeared on no tab at all, so they were added to the waiting list — without that an approved post would have vanished and looked deleted, but it means the waiting count rises on first load for posts that were always there. | 2026-08-19 owner direction after the T11 merge: a post the copy check held, and a brand a partner submitted, both sat in a queue that nothing in the product could empty | (this commit)

Anyone who typed the URL saw it, and so did every non-qualifying homepage survey submitter, because `SURVEY_REDIRECTS` sends both DOWNSELL and MANUAL_REVIEW there. The claim, the calendar block and the "check your email for your calendar invite" line are now all gated on the same `fh_booking_v1` key the .ics button already used; the default markup is the no-booking wording, so a visitor with JavaScript off sees the honest version. `SURVEY_REDIRECTS` was deliberately NOT changed — with the page honest, a downsell visitor now reads a true sentence, and inventing a new downsell destination is a step no `-intended.md` names. (3) The **application promised a second step that was never built**. `02a-apply-top.html` hard-coded "Application · Step 1 of 2"; grep proves the string "Step 2 of 2" exists nowhere in the repo, and the real shape is contact, then eight ClickFunnels survey cards, then book. The false counter is removed rather than a step invented. (4) The **incomplete-application nudge chased people who had finished**. Its completion check read `events` by `client_id`, but `api/public/survey-submit.mjs` emitted `entry.captured` and `survey.submitted` with no client at all, so the check could never succeed and the "they completed it" branch was dead code. Both halves are fixed: the endpoint now resolves-or-creates the client and stamps both events with the id, and the check also matches the email inside the payload so rows written before today still resolve. Note the job itself was already switched back on in `f3fb9a7`; the audit finding that it was deregistered is stale. Honesty pass on the education pages: the claims that a student portal exists, that login credentials are issued, that lessons are available immediately, and a decorative progress bar reading 40% complete, are all reworded or removed — none of `courses`, `lessons`, `modules` or `enrollments` has ever existed in `db/migrations`, and `public/education/learn/` is a plain page saying so rather than a faked player. GAPS recorded, not reconciled: (1) **no `-intended.md` file names the apply funnel, the thank-you page or education at all** — the 2026-08-18 gap audit scored both G4a and G4d "ground truth MISSING", so every verdict in this thread is currently unscoreable against anything hand-written, and an agent may not write an `-intended.md` to close that; (2) the apply, booking and thank-you pages are hosted by ClickFunnels, so the two fragment fixes change nothing on the live site until a person pastes them into the ClickFunnels editor — merging this branch does not ship them; (3) the 555-phone rejection on the apply form is ClickFunnels' own validator, the words "invalid country code" appear nowhere in this repo, and no file here can change it; (4) nothing takes payment for an education program and no CRM screen lists the enrollment rows, so a human must work them by hand; (5) `src/config/offers.mjs` has no key for `credit-mastery` or `capital-strategy`, the two programs the page actually sells, so there is nothing for the pay/unlock chain to grant and no price is stored on the row; (6) the education pages still show `support@fundhubeducation.com`, a domain unrelated to the rest of the site — if that mailbox does not exist, refund requests are going nowhere. | 2026-08-19 live re-walk: the thank-you page told anyone who typed the URL that their call was booked, the application claimed a step 2 that does not exist, and the $5,000 education enrolment form told people they were enrolled while saving nothing | (this commit)
2026-08-19 | client (+ role-owner, role-sales-manager, role-closer, role-funding-advisor, role-inquiry-remover, affiliate, white-label, README) | **COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED** — Client Portal and welcome video, T1 of the 2026-08-18 fix batch. **One route added: `GET/HEAD /api/content/welcome-video`, gated `staff, client`** (`requirePrincipal(req,res,["staff","client"],{db})` then a separate `requireRole` for the staff branch). A client is pinned to their own session on it: `?client_id=` is not read for a client principal, the same shape as `api/read/portal-summary.mjs`. The bytes are served on a signed link with no session at all, because a `<video src>` cannot carry an Authorization header — the same trade `api/documents/[id].mjs` already makes. **The bigger half of this work changed no route and no gate:** `public/portal-login.html` never stored the `account` object that `api/auth/magic-link-verify.mjs` has always returned, so a client who signed in from an emailed link reached the portal with no file id at all — and `public/app/client-portal.html` took an early `return` that skipped every read below it. Proven live on https://fundhub.ai as the real client on 2026-08-19: before, "We could not load your file." and **zero** `/api/read/*` requests; after, six requests, all 200, and the dispute card's real legal wording in place of "Sign in to load the legal wording" shown to somebody who was signed in. The card's "You already signed" was investigated and is **correct, not stuck** — it is a fresh server read of the same validity predicate the soft-pull gate uses. Also: `api/content/tiles.mjs`'s bare catches now forgive exactly one SQLSTATE each (42703 for the missing price column, 42P01 for the missing video tables) so a grant refusal or an RLS deny stops arriving on screen as "0 videos"; the false "Welcome videos are not stored in the database yet" is gone (171 shipped long ago); a save that committed is no longer reported as failed when only the re-read broke; and the portal's dead "Download ↓" button, which never had a click handler and had no client-readable endpoint to call, no longer renders. GAPS recorded, not reconciled: (1) **nothing in the code maps a client to a `content_tier_map.tier_code`** — the Content screen offers product codes, `clients.outcome_tier` is the CRS ladder and is not one, and the portal's tier chip is hardcoded markup; `tierCodeForClient()` therefore returns null with the reason `no_tier_rule_in_code` and every client falls through to the `default` mapping the Content screen already offers, which is an owner decision left visible in code rather than invented; (2) **no `-intended.md` names a client reading their own chat history**, so `GET /api/chat/portal-thread` was designed and deliberately NOT built — a client can send a message and can never see it again, and a staff reply never reaches them; (3) no `-intended.md` names a client-facing view of their credit score, inquiries, funding applications or dispute letters, so all four stay staff-only and unbuilt; (4) the portal's upload card holds a **third** copy of the same id resolver, so a magic-link client still reads "Uploads are off" — reported, not fixed, because closing it needs either a second session round trip or DOM listener-ordering tricks; (5) the two pre-existing untraceable gates (`finance/crs-pull`, `gifts/message-blaster`) are still UNVERIFIED and no third was added. | 2026-08-19 live audit: a client clicked the sign-in link in their email, the site said they were signed in, and the page said "We could not load your file" — with the welcome video, the six offer tiles and the signature box all dead behind the same one missing line | (this commit)
2026-08-19 | white-label (+ every -actual.md: 176 routes to 179) | Marketing suite, T11 of the 2026-08-18 fix batch. **Three routes added.** `POST /api/brand/review` (`api/brand/review.mjs`) — a partner submits their OWN brand for review via `requirePrincipal`, and only an owner or admin may approve or reject, as a SEPARATE `requireRole` call because `requireAuth` silently ignores a `roles` key. `GET /api/social/channels` and `GET/POST /api/social/settings`. All three land with a gate shape the extractor recognises, deliberately: two routes (`finance/crs-pull`, `gifts/message-blaster`) already sit in the UNVERIFIED list and T11 must not join them — checked, the list is still exactly those two. **Brand Studio never loaded the brand it was about to save.** `public/app/brand-studio.html` loaded `data.js` with `defer` and then ran its wiring during parse, so `FHData` did not exist yet: in org mode the block returned in silence, in partner mode it threw `FHData is not defined`, which also killed the status-bar block further down. Proven live 2026-08-19 as `partner@fundhub.ai`: all five boxes `value:""`, **no `/api/partner-brand` GET ever made**, and Save answering "Legal entity is required" with zero network traffic while the server held `entity_name: "E2E WL Click17 Co"`. Now inside `DOMContentLoaded`, matching `finance-os.html` and `client-control-panel.html`. **The Creative Factory Enqueue card was invisible to partners for a reason nobody guessed:** the card's own footnote links to `campaign-manager.html`, and `public/app/shell.js` hides `a.closest('.card')` for any screen a role may not open — so one prose link took the whole primary-action card, and the Approve/Reject row with it, off the page. Fixed inside the screen; `shell.js` is T0's and was not touched. **Creative enqueue was rejected by the database for an owner:** `generation_jobs.requested_by` is a foreign key to `accounts(id)` and the handler wrote a staff id, which are separate tables that can never coincide, so the insert raised and the handler answered 500. `db/migrations/241` supersedes 045 with the typed-requester shape 077 already uses (kind + one FK per kind); the old column is kept, nullable and never written again, because NULL means unknown and must survive. **BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, the biggest in this thread:** `db/migrations/240` adds `awaiting_approval` to `social_posts` and redefines 049's screen-gate trigger so a post the compliance engine marks `needs_approval` can only reach the holding pen, never `queued`. Before this, `src/social/scheduler.mjs` treated `needs_approval` and `passed` identically and 049's own trigger allowed it, so a post the engine said needed a person went out anyway. `src/compliance/screen.mjs` returns `needs_approval` for `credit_repair` **unconditionally** — its comment says the gate cannot be turned off — and **nothing anywhere writes `social_posts.approved_at`**, so from this branch forward every credit-repair social post stops and stays stopped. That is the safe direction and it is deliberate, but it is a stop, and it is recorded here as one. 240 also moves already-queued posts into the pen, but ONLY those whose screening said `needs_approval` and never `passed`; a `passed` post is left where it is, a `posting` post is left alone, and a queued post with no screening row at all is reported rather than guessed at. Nothing is deleted and no post is sent that would not have been sent a minute ago — the migration can only stop a send. 2026-08-19 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | **COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED** — Client self-serve identity capture, T3-11 of the 2026-08-18 fix batch. **No route added, no role gate moved, every `-actual.md` byte-identical** — `npm run journeys:check` reports up to date. The change rides on the GET side of the existing `/api/consent/capture`, which was already routed and already gated. **Owner decision, 2026-08-19:** identity capture is client self-serve; staff never see, type or handle a Social Security number. That decision cost nothing to honour, because the client-facing form already existed — `public/app/soft-pull-approve.html` is a public signed-link page with no CRM shell and no staff nav (`src/http/app-nav-matches-shell.test.mjs:36`), its form already collects name, SSN, date of birth and address, and `api/soft-pull-approve.mjs` already stores the identity AND captures consent in one submission. The only missing piece was a way for staff to hand that link over, so that is all that was built: the consent GET now also returns a freshly signed link with its expiry, and a plain true/false on whether an identity is already on file. **A boolean and a timestamp are the entire permitted surface** — no SSN, no last four, no date of birth, no address may cross that endpoint, and the test asserting it is a deny-list scan over the whole serialised body rather than a check of three named fields, so a field added later cannot slip past it. Proven by seeding a real identity, invoking the handler and scanning every value in the response. The link is minted for a staff session only — it is a bearer credential and a signed-in client has no use for a link to a page they can already reach. Both extras are soft-pull only: a dispute-authorization request never reads the identity table at all. Two failure paths fail soft rather than blanking the screen: a missing signing key returns the link as null with a sentence naming the setting, and a failed identity read degrades to "we did not find out", which the screen renders differently from "we hold nothing". On the client page, three fixes and no redesign: it was showing the internal string `soft-pull-v1` to a consumer as a heading, a plain "what happens next" list now appears before the form rather than only in small print under the button, and the address is trimmed so a box holding one space cannot read as filled in. This REVERSES the engineering decision recorded at `src/handlers/contract-signed.mjs:41-48` ("Two writers for one permission is how a revocation stops biting") — **owner-set 2026-08-19**, and that earlier reasoning was taken as the specification: a withdrawal dated after the signature wins forever, and a replayed event cannot write a second row. (2) `POST /api/consent/capture` was missing the `org_id` check both its siblings run, so staff at one company could record consent naming another company's client; it now matches `api/finance/crs-pull.mjs`. (3) The demo seed wrote credit scores under two nested keys no reader looks at, so a client seeded as FULL_FUNDING read $0 and score 0 — the seed now writes the real bureau shape `result.scores` / `scoreModels` / `bureaus`, plus the six `clients.custom_fields` underwriting counts nothing has ever written, and the same client now reads score 731 and $939,500. The reader was NOT bent to fit the seed: `result.scores` is what a real pull stores (`src/finance/crs-map.mjs:196-223`) and the seed exists to imitate a real pull. (4) A bureau pull can now be rehearsed end to end without contacting a bureau (`simulate: true`), stamped in five places, inventing no numbers and writing no outcome tier. 2026-08-18 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | Bank-application launcher, proxy and lenders, T8 of the 2026-08-18 fix batch. **Every `-actual.md` regenerated byte-identical and that is the correct result:** no route was added or removed and no role gate moved — all eight T8 handlers were already in the ROUTES map, verified one by one, and `api/proxy/launch.mjs` keeps the `requireAuth` + separate `requireRole` shape the extractor recognises. Three real defects fixed, each proven broken on https://fundhub.ai on 2026-08-18 before it was touched. (1) Pressing Apply on a bank application answered 503 and recorded **nothing** — `src/proxy/launch.mjs` threw on missing Oxylabs credentials BEFORE `insertProxySession`, so `proxy_sessions` stayed at 0 rows and the attempt was invisible to the audit trail. The check is gone; `launchCredentials()` already returns the same `oxylabs_credentials_missing` error and the same 503 further down, AFTER the pending row exists, so a refused launch now leaves a row at `status='failed'` with `error_code` set. Behaviour change worth its own line: a client with no city or state on file now reports `client_location_missing` instead of the credentials error, because that check now runs first — that is the more useful of the two answers and it is deliberate. (2) The failure box on the Apply screen showed an error code and nothing else, so the manual fallback the audit went looking for never appeared. It now carries a plain `next_step` per failure and states that browser routing is **NOT** active and the application was **not** opened. It deliberately does **not** print host, port, username or password on a failure: the session never came up, nothing was geo-verified, and printing settings that were never checked would be a lie about where the bank thinks you are. (3) `src/banking/accounts-store.mjs` built `ON CONFLICT` without repeating either partial index's `WHERE` predicate (081 line 123, 103 line 162), so Postgres found no arbiter and every provider sync died 42P10 on its very first account — no bank account has ever reached `bank_accounts` through that path against a real database. Fake-database tests could not see it. Also fixed: `api/lenders.mjs` could answer `ok:true` with `lender:null`, which the Lenders screen wrote straight back into its row cache, costing the row its id and breaking that row's next Save; it now returns 409 `save_failed`. GAPS recorded, not reconciled: (1) **no `-intended.md` file describes screen behaviour at all** — they record which role can open which route and nothing more — so nothing hand-written covers the Apply failure copy, the Lenders import, or a connect-a-bank control, and the wording decisions above are recorded here and in code comments instead; (2) `read/proxy-sessions` is routed and gated {owner, funding_advisor} but **no page in `public/` fetches it**, so even a successful proxy session is invisible on screen — T8 did not add a page, because that is a new CRM surface and the owner's call; (3) real bank linking stays an open, honest seam — `src/banking/plaid.mjs` refuses with `not_configured` / `not_implemented` and never returns an empty account list as if it were the truth, and its own header says an agent must not close it; (4) the `lenders` table is still empty for the org, so lender matching honestly returns 0 — the import path is now proven end to end against a scratch Postgres with obviously-fake fixture rows, but no real lender was invented to make the count non-zero. | 2026-08-18 live audit: pressing launch on the bank-application tool failed with a server error and left no record it had ever been tried, and no client could ever match a lender | (this commit)

2026-08-18 | (route table changed on every page — see below) | Bookings, calendar and inbound webhooks, T7 of the 2026-08-18 fix batch. **One route added: `GET /api/bookings`, gated `staff`** (`api/bookings.mjs` via `requirePrincipal(req,res,["staff"],{db})`, all SQL in `src/bookings/store.mjs`, org-scoped on every query). A booking is now stored as a booking: `db/migrations/225_bookings.sql` creates `bookings` and backfills it from the `booking.created` / `.cancelled` / `.noshow` events already recorded, keyed on (company, provider booking id) — deliberately NOT on the vendor label, because the same appointment reported under two labels was becoming two rows. `starts_at`/`ends_at` stay NULLable: a payload with no start time is unknown, not epoch. The four booking handlers in `src/handlers/comms.mjs` stop hard-coding `sourceWorkflow: "calcom"` and record the true origin — measured live 2026-08-18, 27 `booking.created` events came from ClickFunnels and **0 have ever come from Cal.com**, while all 16 booking tasks read "calcom". The same migration repairs those 16 rows, but only where a real event proves the true source; an unmatched row keeps the label it has. **BEHAVIOUR CHANGE worth a line of its own:** a cancellation or no-show whose booking id matches nothing no longer invents a booking row — it is logged as a miss, which is what the one-time backfill already did, so the live handler and the backfill finally agree. `src/sales/call-outcomes.mjs` now finds a closer's unlogged calls by title (`Strategy session%`) instead of by the vendor word, which is what stops the relabel emptying the Closer Call, My Numbers and Sales Floor lists. 2026-08-18 | (no role journey changed — recorded deliberately) | Messaging, opt-out and inbound mail, T5 of the 2026-08-18 fix batch. The generator classes it exactly where it belongs — with `/api/contracts/sign` and `/api/soft-pull-approve` under "genuinely open, no sign-in needed, reachable by anyone and not by this journey in particular" — so every `-actual.md` moved from 174 routes to 175 and from 15 open routes to 16, and NO role's can-reach or blocked list changed. That is the correct result: this is a page a person opens while signed OUT, and the signed token in the URL is the credential. Also changed, and invisible to these pages because they track routes and gates rather than behaviour: an inbound email whose whole body is STOP now records an opt-out on the email channel; Mailgun's own `unsubscribed` event does the same instead of being ignored; and an inbound text from a client whose stored phone is not in +1 format now resolves to that client instead of nobody. GAPS recorded, not reconciled: (1) no `-intended.md` names an unsubscribe step, because none of them describes any public no-session page — they describe API routes reachable by a signed-in principal, so `/unsubscribe.html` is outside what they cover, the same way `/contract.html` and `/portal-login.html` already are; (2) the route counts in every `-intended.md` are still the 2026-08-02 numbers and now trail actual by more than this change alone; (3) no `-intended.md` describes opt-out or consent-withdrawal behaviour on any channel, so nothing hand-written covers whether an emailed STOP should stop email — that judgment is recorded here and in the code comments instead. | 2026-08-18 live audit: a client emailed STOP, it was filed as an ordinary message, `opt_outs` held zero rows, and 173 email templates promised an unsubscribe link that did not exist and pointed at a page that 404'd | (this commit)
2026-08-18 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | Database and security, T16 of the 2026-08-18 fix batch. Three things changed and none of them is a route or a role gate. (1) Six credit-dispute tables were locked shut on the live database — the row lock was switched on outside the migration files and no key was ever attached, which in Postgres means deny everything to the ordinary app login. The app read zero rows and could not write at all, and neither failure raised an error, so the whole credit-dispute feature has been quietly doing nothing. `db/migrations/200_dispute_rls_policies.sql` unlocks all six and, importantly, DECLARES the lock state in a file so a test database and the live database finally describe the same thing — the existing guard could never see this because the drift did not live in the files it reads. `201_no_bare_rls_sweep.sql` is the standing net for the next table that drifts the same way; this is the third time this class of bug has shipped (109, 154, now 160/161). (2) Deleting a demo client failed with a server error and left the client behind while destroying its own data first. The delete list was hand-written, named 3 of the 16 tables that can refuse, and silenced every error but the last. It now reads the list from the database itself, runs as one atomic statement, and reports which table refused. (3) `202_client_fk_indexes.sql` indexed 23 columns that point at a client and had no index, which is what made that delete slow enough to time out. **Every `-actual.md` regenerated byte-identical, and that is the correct result:** no route was added or removed, no role gate moved, and `api/demo/simulate.mjs` keeps the exact `requireAuth` + separate `requireRole` shape the journey extractor recognises — deliberately, because two other routes already sit in the extractor's UNVERIFIED list and this one must not join them. Journeys track routes and gates, not database row locks. GAPS recorded, not reconciled: (1) no `-intended.md` file describes the database, row locks, or demo teardown at all, so nothing hand-written covers any of this work — the audit that found it used a board order, not a journey; (2) 147 of 175 tables carry an allow-all row policy, so one shared app login can read every client's file — that is an owner-level decision about the product's security model, deliberately NOT changed here, and it is recorded on the fix board; (3) `affiliate_payout_lines` carries a guard trigger with no demo exemption, so a demo client whose commission landed in a paid affiliate run still cannot be torn down — it now fails loudly by name instead of silently. | 2026-08-18 live audit: the credit-dispute feature had nowhere to read or write, and pressing delete on a demo client errored and left it there | (this commit)
2026-08-18 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | **COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED** — Background jobs and automations, T6 of the 2026-08-18 fix batch. Two workflows that existed on disk but were never served are now served — `s-02-incomplete-survey-nudge` (on `entry.captured`, 400 rows and nothing listening) and `inquiry-call-sweeper` (cron `*/15`) — an **owner decision taken 2026-08-19**; `src/workflows/index.test.mjs` now fails if any `inngest.createFunction` on disk is neither served nor named in `DELIBERATELY_UNSERVED` with a reason, so 53-written/51-served cannot hide again. `GET /api/read/workflows` stopped reporting `engine_active` from a single env-var check and stopped calling a workflow "live" because an event row shares its name; statuses are now `scheduled` / `trigger_seen` / `trigger_never_seen` / `no_emitter`, each row carries `can_start_today` with a plain reason naming the switched-off secret, and `run_records_available` is always false because nothing in this database records a workflow finishing. `POST /api/journeys/run` now excludes demo journey rows (they have no start event, fired nothing, and their presence stopped the six real journeys being walked at all) and reports the skipped keys; the runner passes `skipInngest: true` so the always-rolled-back harness can no longer start real automations over the network; `PUT /api/journeys` validates node config per step type. **Every `-actual.md` regenerated byte-identical, and that is the correct result:** no route was added or removed and no role gate moved — `journeys`, `journeys/run` and `read/workflows` keep the gates they had (`FINANCE` after `requireAuth`, `STAFF` for the registry read). These pages track routes and gates, not workflow registration or screen wording. GAPS recorded, not reconciled: (1) no `-intended.md` names the Automations or Journeys screen behaviour at all — they describe route reach only — so nothing hand-written covers what either screen should say, which is why the wording changes were made against the audit evidence rather than against an intended journey; (2) `role-owner-intended.md` does not say who may run a journey test, so the existing owner/admin gate was left exactly as found; (3) the six journey keys the runner knows (`client`, `setter`, `closer`, `advisor`, `affiliate`, `partner`) still do not match the eight journeys these pages track — `role-owner` and `role-inquiry-remover` have no tree, recorded here and in the runner's own header, not invented. | 2026-08-18 live audit: the Automations screen told the owner 49 of 51 jobs were live when the count had risen only because auditors fired test events, two clock jobs were labelled dead while working, and the journey test reported "0 of 51 automations reached" while quietly able to fire live automations | (this commit)

Route lives at `/api/documents-upload`, not `/api/documents/upload`, because src/http/routes.test.mjs forbids an exact ROUTES key that would sit under the `documents/` prefix branch (the signed-download route) and depend on lookup order. 2026-08-02 | client, role-owner, role-sales-manager, role-closer, role-funding-advisor, role-inquiry-remover, affiliate, white-label | Launch-readiness pass on the staff reply inbox. No route added or removed, so the totals hold at 80 — but three gates changed behaviour. (1) OWNERS NO LONGER CLOCK IN: `requireActiveShift` gained an opt-in `exempt` option and all three shift-gated endpoints (api/messages, api/inquiries, api/tasks) pass SUPER_ROLES, closing the question ACTIVE-SHIFT-ROLLOUT.md §3c and §4 explicitly refused to guess. An exempt caller's work is recorded against a NULL shift, which is the honest value rather than an invented one. (2) HELD MESSAGES NOW ACTUALLY SEND: netlify/functions/staff-message-sweeper.mjs, a Netlify scheduled function every five minutes, scoped to messages a person wrote. (3) The texting-hours rule is stated on the compose box before anything is typed | Owner decisions, 2026-08-02, plus two defects found while acting on them. The overnight hold was enforced and announced nowhere, so the first anyone knew of it was a reply that did not go — and the screen's promise that a held text 'will go out on its own' was untrue, because nothing in this repository had ever called the dispatcher on a clock. The sweeper is deliberately NOT the Inngest one: registering that needs INNGEST_EVENT_KEY, which CLAUDE.md §11 reserves for the owner because it makes 47 workflows live, and it would drain thousands of stale workflow-queued messages onto real people. A migration repointing SMS to Twilio was written on the owner's stated direction and then WITHDRAWN — acting on it surfaced A2P 10DLC, which three places in this repo already record as the reason Twilio ships disabled; unregistered carrier traffic is filtered silently, and a migration runs unattended. 118 is a documented no-op carrying the switch for a human. The related real defect is unfixed and named: the GHL relay addresses by ghl_contact_id, which is NULL for every client created since the cutover, so those customers cannot receive a text reply. Also: the inbox list query was rebuilt so it stops scanning the whole conversations table per page load, the needs-reply tab moved server-side, and Playwright landed — CLAUDE.md §6 gate 4, listed as required since the file was written and never once runnable. 18 browser tests, blocking in CI. Full spec in docs/REPLY-INBOX-SPEC.md | (this commit)

The staff reply inbox is built end to end: an inbound SMS or a client's own email now finds-or-creates that client's conversation for that channel and threads the message onto it; messaging.html stops being sample markup and becomes a real inbox (conversation list newest-first with a needs-reply marker, thread view, compose box, client context panel), and a staff reply goes out through the existing dispatcher and the existing compliance gate. `messages.sender_staff_id` (120, renumbered from 117 during merge — main already had 117/118/119) records who wrote it.
