2026-08-02 | all eight | No route or screen change — the fix is underneath. Now that the outbound queue actually drains, two things that were safe only because nothing sent are not safe any more, and both are closed. (1) A journey run mints fake clients and puts real-looking messages in the queue; the run-level fence refuses a run when the company's routing can reach the outside world, but the QUEUE OUTLIVES THE ROUTING — run against the memory provider today, connect Mailgun next week, and the next drain mails invented people. The dispatcher now refuses a synthetic client outright whenever the resolved provider can transmit, permanently and with no override. (2) `messaging_settings.alert_email` was collected and never read; three or more failures in one pass now file a task for the owner saying emails are not going out, once per company per day | The fence's own header recorded this as an open question left for whoever plugged sending in — that was the previous commit, so it is answered here rather than left as a note. The rule is the INVERSE of the old one: not "only test clients may be sent to", which refused every real client and is why the fence was unwired in the first place, but "a test client may never be sent to for real." The alert is a task and NOT an email on purpose — it fires exactly when sending is broken, so an emailed alert is the one message guaranteed not to arrive. Also fixed the same committed-DDL race in the outbox test cleanup that was fixed in the hiring tests | (this commit)
2026-08-02 | all eight | The platform can send email at all. One route added — `POST /api/messages-outbound` (`status` is STAFF; `dispatch`, `settings`, `email_invoice` and `email_invoice_backlog` are owner/admin), so route totals and role reach move by one everywhere. Routed as `messages-outbound` rather than `messages` because the already-merged staff-reply-inbox branch claims `POST /api/messages` for a staff reply — both branches independently wrote a handler at `api/messages.mjs` for two unrelated features, and this one was renamed at merge time (see docs/MERGE-LOG.md section 3). One workflow joined the registry (`message-dispatch-sweeper`, 48 -> 49). The change underneath every journey page is that the outbound queue finally drains: twenty-six workflows have been calling `sendTemplated()` and writing queued `messages` rows since they were written, and NOTHING HAS EVER SENT ONE. Invoices were worse — `invoices.status` has included 'sent' since 017 and no code ever emailed one, so two workflows have been raising invoices nobody was told about. `invoices.emailed_at` now records what actually left, separately from what we claimed | The owner asked for invoices by email and for any other sending gaps, and rejected sending as a hidden global switch: "it's not like the workflow is something that we turn on... it should be an option, the ability to set it up... it's a fucking CRM." So the gate moved rather than disappearing — db/migrations/126_outbound_switch.sql gives each company its own `outbound_enabled`, a daily send cap, and a record of who changed it, all visible in the CRM on the ops-admin Outbound Mail panel. INNGEST_EVENT_KEY is still unset and still the owner's to set (CLAUDE.md §11); registering the sweeper sends nothing without it, and the same drain runs today from a button. The compliance gate, the daily cap and the absence of provider credentials are all unchanged. Flagged COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED — the invoice email is fee-timing copy. Full decision record in docs/CONTRACTS-SPEC.md Part 4 | (this commit)
2026-08-02 | all eight | Contracts now really send, and chase. No new routes: `/api/contracts` gained `run_reminders`, and one workflow joined the registry (`contract-chaser`, 47 -> 48), so every journey page's route totals and role reach are unchanged. What changed underneath them is that the outbound path finally has a CALLER — src/messaging/dispatch.mjs has said in its own header since it was written that "dispatchDue() runs when something calls it, and today nothing does", and that was true of the whole platform, not of contracts in particular. Pressing Send now queues one message per signer carrying that person's own link and hands THOSE ROWS ONLY to the dispatcher | The owner asked why it did not send, and pointed out that chasing is just a workflow. Both fair. Dispatching by id rather than calling dispatchDue() keeps the blast radius to the one contract: draining the whole queue is the switch CLAUDE.md §11 reserves to the owner, and it stays unthrown. The chaser IS registered — which is not the same act, because Inngest invokes nothing until INNGEST_EVENT_KEY is set — and it also runs today without Inngest through the new endpoint and a button on the Contracts screen. The message dispatch sweeper deliberately stays out of the registry and its guard test still passes | (this commit)
2026-08-02 | all eight | The contract generator becomes a signing service. No new routes — the three added earlier the same day gained actions instead (`upload_template`, `save_fields`, `create_client` on `/api/contracts`; file serving and signer lists on `/api/read/contracts`; per-signer links, file fetch and decline on `/api/contracts/sign`), so the route totals and role reach on every journey are unchanged. What moved on these pages is the client-facing surface: `/api/contracts/sign` now serves the DOCUMENT ITSELF to an unauthenticated caller, and remains one of the six genuinely open routes. A PDF can be uploaded, boxes dragged onto its pages, and the whole thing sent to several people who sign IN AN ORDER — under a sequential contract the second signer cannot open the document at all until the first has signed, refused at the endpoint rather than hidden on the screen | The owner asked for DocuSign inside the CRM ("we upload PDFs and do the contract thing... put in fields, like date, name, address, whatever, from the client information") with questions closed, so every remaining decision was taken by the build agent and written into docs/CONTRACTS-SPEC.md Part 2. Two dependencies were added under that grant: pdf-lib to fill and flatten PDFs, and pdf.js vendored into public/ so pages render with no build step and no third-party host. Storage went through src/documents/store.mjs, the same document store every other upload in this codebase already used — an earlier draft of this branch proposed its own competing storage design (a Postgres-backed fallback provider, auto-selected from BLOB_READ_WRITE_TOKEN/DATABASE_URL), which conflicted with the already-merged uploads branch's simpler design and was discarded at merge time rather than kept alongside it (docs/MERGE-LOG.md section 3). Additive throughout: source_kind defaults to 'text', and all 66 tests written for the typed-copy contracts pass unchanged | (this commit)
2026-08-02 | all eight | Three routes added — `POST /api/contracts` (staff write surface: template authoring, drafts, preview, send, void), `GET /api/read/contracts` (the library, the queue, and one contract with its frozen wording) and `GET/POST /api/contracts/sign`. The third is the important one on these pages: it is the second route in the whole platform that takes NO SESSION AT ALL, so it joins `/api/documents/<id>` in the "genuinely open" list on every journey and the open-route count moves from 5 to 6. Auth on it is the HMAC in the link, not a principal — which is why it reads as open here and is not. One new CRM screen (contracts.html, in every sidebar, `ROLE_SETS.STAFF` with an owner/admin-only wording card inside it) and one client page outside the CRM (`/contract.html`, no shell.js, no session) | The contract generator: a staff member writes a contract once, sends it to any client, and gets it back signed, with no developer involved at any step. Built against 030's documents/document_versions rather than a new immutability scheme — the rendered wording is frozen at send and hash-checked at signature against `document_versions.checksum`, so a contract altered after sending cannot be signed at all. Regenerated with `npm run journeys` from the code, per CLAUDE.md §4, not hand-edited. NOTE THE GAP THIS DOES NOT CLOSE: there is no `*-intended.md` anywhere in this directory, so nothing was checked against an intended journey before building — reported rather than reconciled.
